### demgen


#### [blocker] DEMGEN-01 — 2D contour layers silently produce an all-NoData DEM reported as "DEM 생성 완료!" — including the plugin's own 등고선 추출 output

- 위치: `tools/dem_generator_dialog.py` :883-898
- 문제: When none of five exact, case-sensitive field names is found the code falls back unconditionally to valueSource=1 (QgsInterpolator::ValueZ) — geometry Z — without ever checking that the layer is 3D. QGIS rejects every such feature: QgsInterpolator::addVerticesToCache does `case Z: if (!geom.constGet()->is3D()) return false;` (qgsinterpolator.cpp) and QgsTinInterpolator::insertData does `case ValueZ: if (!g.constGet()->is3D()) return 3;` — and QgsTinInterpolator::initialize() ignores that return value. The triangulation ends up empty, QgsGridFileWriter writes -9999 for every pixel (340_qgsgridfilewriter.cpp: `float32Row[col] = -9999;`), the file exists, so the plugin publishes it and reports success. Nothing warns. The detection list is also strictly weaker than the plugin's own Kriging lis …
- 실패 시나리오: Closed loop inside this plugin. User runs 등고선 추출 on a DEM: contour_extractor_dialog.py:278-287 calls gdal:contour with FIELD_NAME='ELEV' and CREATE_3D=False, producing a 2D LineString GeoPackage named "등고선_5m". User opens DEM 생성; populate_layers (line 389) AUTO-CHECKS that layer because its name contains '등고선'. Run with TIN - Linear. Every feature is rejected as non-3D, the GeoTIFF is written entirely as -9999, atomic_publish_file promotes it, addRasterLayer adds it, and line 1105 …
- 제안 수정: Before building interp_data, when z_field_idx < 0, test the geometry for Z (e.g. QgsWkbTypes.hasZ(merged_layer.wkbType()) or a sample feature's geometry.constGet().is3D()). If the layer is 2D, abort with a message naming the problem ("표고 필드를 찾을 수 없고 3D 좌표도 없습니다") instead of falling through to ValueZ. #### [major] DEMGEN-02 — Kriging (Lite) does not honour the input spot heights: a 30.00 m surveyed benchmark comes back as 25.05 m at its own location

- 위치: `tools/kriging_lite.py` :428, 480, 495
- 문제: The covariance matrix uses C(0)=partial_sill+nugget on the diagonal, but the right-hand side cvec is taken from the continuous function, which returns partial_sill (nugget omitted) at h=0. The two sides therefore use different definitions of C(0). Ordinary Kriging as published by Matheron 1963 / Cressie 1993 — the citation carried in the method name 'Kriging (Lite, Ordinary)' (dem_generator_dialog.py:71-75) and in REFERENCES.md — is an exact interpolator: at a sample location λ=e_i and σ²=0. Mixing the conventions turns it into filtered / measurement-error kriging, which smooths the data away, while the variance formula (line 495) still uses the observation-level C(0)=psill+nugget, so it is not the variance of the quantity actually being predicted either. Nothing in the dialog, README.md o …
- 실패 시나리오: Executed against the repo's real module (qgis.core/osgeo.gdal stubbed, kriging_lite.py byte-identical). Input: 49 surveyed spot heights on a 10 m grid over 0-60 m, all 0.00 m except one benchmark of 30.00 m at (30,30); value_field='Elevation'; extent (-2.5,-2.5,62.5,62.5); pixel_size=5.0 so a cell centre lands exactly on (30,30); neighbors=16. auto_params yields nugget=0.9184, partial_sill=17.449, range=30.0. The cell whose centre is exactly the benchmark gets 25.0451 m — a -4.955 m error at a point where the surveyed value was supplied — and the variance raster reports 1.6851 m² there, i.e. ± …
- 제안 수정: Either (a) make it the Ordinary Kriging it claims to be: build cvec with the full covariance, i.e. set cvec[dist0 == 0] = partial_sill + nugget, which restores exactness and σ²=0 at data locations; or (b) keep the filtering behaviour but say so — rename the method (e.g. 'Kriging (Lite, filtered / 측정오차 모델)'), use C(0)=partial_sill in the variance at line 495 so the variance matches the signal actually predicted, and state in the dialog hint and README that input spot heights are smoothed rather t …

#### [major] DEMGEN-03 — The DXF layer-code filter is applied to ANY selected layer that has a field named "Layer", using checkbox defaults the user may never have opened

- 위치: `tools/dem_generator_dialog.py` :821-827, 877-879
- 문제: The DXF code table is checked by default (DXF_LAYER_INFO defaults True for F0017111, F0017114, F0027217 at lines 83/86/89) and get_selected_layer_codes deliberately returns checked codes from BOTH eras regardless of which tab is visible. The resulting subset string is then applied to whatever layer the user picked in the list widget, on the sole condition that the layer happens to own a field called 'Layer'. CAD-derived shapefiles and GeoPackages carry exactly that field ('Layer' survives the 10-char shapefile truncation), so a layer that has nothing to do with the modern NGII code set gets filtered by the modern code set. The comment claims 'No silent auto-excludes: use exactly what the user selected in the table', but the table's defaults are a selection the user never made.
- 실패 시나리오: User has 등고선.shp converted from an old (구) 수치지형도, with a 'Layer' column holding '7111' and '7114' plus '7217' spot heights. They check it in the layer list and never touch the DXF code table, which is on the default 현행 tab with F0017111/F0017114/F0027217 checked. Run: the subset '"Layer" IN (\'F0017111\',\'F0017114\',\'F0027217\')' matches zero features, the layer's extent becomes null, and qgis:tininterpolation dies on math.ceil(NaN) — the user sees "처리 중 오류: cannot convert float NaN to integer", which names neither the layer nor the filter. The silent variant is worse: a dataset that stores …
- 제안 수정: Only apply the code filter to layers the plugin itself loaded from DXF (it already tracks them in self.loaded_dxf_layers, and tags their names with '_DEM용'), or gate it behind an explicit checkbox. At minimum, compare featureCount() before and after setSubsetString and refuse to continue — or warn with the actual numbers — when the filter drops features, e.g. "레이어 코드 필터로 1,284개 중 0개만 남았습니다".

#### [major] DEMGEN-04 — A stale DEM left at the output path from an earlier run is re-loaded and reported as this run's result

- 위치: `tools/dem_generator_dialog.py` :1082-1106
- 문제: os.path.exists(output_path) is treated as proof that this run produced the file, but the publish at line 1083 is conditional on the staged file existing. If the interpolation returned without writing anything, staging_out is never promoted and line 1087 still finds the previous run's raster sitting at output_path. QgsGridFileWriter::writeFile returns 1/2 on a failed raster creation and QgsTinInterpolationAlgorithm ignores that return value, so processing.run yields a truthy results dict with no file behind it. The old raster is then added to the canvas as "생성된 DEM" and stamped via set_archtoolkit_layer_metadata with THIS run's run_id, pixel_size_m, method and algorithm — so the provenance record the plugin's report bundle reads describes parameters that never produced that file.
- 실패 시나리오: User generates /survey/dem.tif with TIN - Linear at 1 m and it succeeds. Later they rerun to the same path with Clough-Tocher at 0.5 m, but the disk fills (or the share went read-only) so GDAL cannot create the staged raster. processing.run returns {'OUTPUT': ...} with no file; the `os.path.exists(staging_out)` test fails; `os.path.exists(output_path)` is True because the January raster is still there. QGIS loads the OLD 1 m linear DEM, the message bar says "DEM 생성 완료!", and the layer now carries archtoolkit/params_json = {"pixel_size_m":0.5,"method":"TIN - Clough-Tocher (곡면)",...}. Every down …
- 제안 수정: Bind success to the publish, not to the destination existing. Set a flag when atomic_publish_file actually runs and require it at line 1087, e.g. `published = False` … `atomic_publish_file(...); published = True` … `if published and os.path.exists(output_path):`. Otherwise fall through to the existing "DEM이 생성되지 않았습니다." branch and say that the file at the output path is from a previous run.

#### [major] DEMGEN-05 — TIN/IDW output pixels are not the requested pixel size, and are not square — but the metadata records the requested size as fact

- 위치: `tools/dem_generator_dialog.py` :1065-1071, 1098
- 문제: PIXEL_SIZE is not the cell size QGIS produces. qgis:tininterpolation and qgis:idwinterpolation use it only to derive counts — `columns = max(ceil(bbox.width()/pixel_size), 1)`, `rows = max(ceil(bbox.height()/pixel_size), 1)` — and QgsGridFileWriter then divides the extent by those counts: `mCellSizeX(extent.width()/nCols)`, `mCellSizeY(extent.height()/nRows)` (340_qgsgridfilewriter.cpp:32-33). The resulting cell size is therefore always ≤ the requested value, X and Y differ from each other, and the dialog ("출력 해상도 (m):", "(권장: 5.0m)") plus the layer metadata (`"pixel_size_m": 5.0`, `units="m"`) state the requested number as if it were the delivered one. The Kriging path in the same dialog does honour pixel_size exactly (kriging_lite.py:327 writes a geotransform of (xmin, px, 0, ymax, 0, -p …
- 실패 시나리오: A trench survey whose contour extent is 18.4 m x 11.2 m, run with the dialog's default 1:5,000 preset (pixel size 5.0 m, set automatically by on_scale_changed). columns = ceil(18.4/5)=4, rows = ceil(11.2/5)=3, so the GeoTIFF is written with 4.600 m x 3.733 m rectangular cells — 25% off in Y and a 23% aspect distortion — while the added layer carries archtoolkit/params_json {"pixel_size_m":5.0} and the user's report says "해상도 5 m". A reviewer opening the file in gdalinfo sees 4.6 x 3.733. On a normal site (346.6 m x 198.5 m at 1 m) the same mechanism gives 0.99885 m x 0.99749 m — small, but the …
- 제안 수정: Either snap the extent before calling the algorithm so the division is exact — expand combined_extent to xmin + ceil(width/px)*px and ymax - ceil(height/px)*px, as kriging_lite already does implicitly — or read the real geotransform back from the published raster (out_layer.rasterUnitsPerPixelX()/Y()) and record those as pixel_size_x_m / pixel_size_y_m in set_archtoolkit_layer_metadata instead of the requested value.

#### [major] DEMGEN-06 — Merging a 2D contour layer with a 3D one silently assigns 0 m elevation to every 2D vertex

- 위치: `tools/dem_generator_dialog.py` :838-845
- 문제: QgsMergeVectorAlgorithm promotes the output WKB type to Z as soon as ANY input has Z (merge340.cpp:139-143) and then pads the others: `if (hasZ && !g.constGet()->is3D()) { g.get()->addZValue(0); }` (merge340.cpp:238-242). The features from the 2D input therefore arrive in the merged GeoPackage with Z=0. QGIS emits an informational 'Found a layer with Z values, upgrading output type' line, but processing.run is called with no feedback object so that message goes nowhere the user will see. Combined with the ValueZ fallback at line 898, those padded vertices are then interpolated as genuine 0 m elevations.
- 실패 시나리오: A site has two contour shapefiles: 등고선_기존.shp (LineStringZ, exported 3D from CAD) and 등고선_보완.shp (plain 2D LineString with an 'ELEV' attribute). Both are auto-checked by populate_layers because their names contain '등고선'. The merge upgrades the output to LineStringZ and writes Z=0 on every vertex of the 2D layer. 'ELEV' is not in the Z-field name list, so valueSource=ValueZ is used and the TIN is built from the real 3D contours plus a full set of contour lines pinned at 0 m. The published DEM has a sea-level crater cut through the site wherever the 2D layer had coverage, and the message bar say …
- 제안 수정: Check the inputs before merging: if valueSource will be ValueZ (no elevation attribute found) and the selected layers are not all 3D, refuse and say which layer lacks Z. Alternatively pass a QgsProcessingFeedback into processing.run and surface its pushInfo/pushWarning lines into the live log dialog that run_process already opens at line 813, so the Z-upgrade warning reaches the user.

#### [minor] DEMGEN-07 — When the Kriging Z field is auto-detected, nothing records which attribute the DEM was actually built from

- 위치: `tools/dem_generator_dialog.py` :910-918, 1006
- 문제: The default combo entry '자동(추천)' carries currentData() == "", so value_field stays None and kriging_lite._auto_value_field (kriging_lite.py:59-91) silently picks the first of twelve candidate names present on the layer — Z_COORD, z_coord, Elevation, ELEVATION, elev, ELEV, height, HEIGHT, 표고, 고도, z, Z — with no type or plausibility test beyond float-convertibility. The name it settled on is never surfaced: the provenance metadata writes "value_field": "" (line 1006 and, for the variance layer, line 1032), the returned info dict's params block (kriging_lite.py:527-533) carries only model/nugget/partial_sill/range/neighbors, and log_message at kriging_lite.py:392-395 logs only the variogram. A layer with both a real 'Elevation' column and a stray integer 'Z' column resolves by list order, not …
- 실패 시나리오: A 표고점 point layer exported from a survey package carries both 'HEIGHT' (the surveyed elevation, metres) and 'Elevation' (an instrument-height column in centimetres, left over from the export). _auto_value_field reaches 'Elevation' before 'height' and kriges the instrument heights. The DEM looks plausible in shape, the message bar says "Kriging 보간 완료!", and archtoolkit/params_json on the layer says value_field "" — so neither the analyst six months later nor the report bundle that reads this metadata can tell which column produced the surface, and the unit error is undiscoverable from the outpu …
- 제안 수정: Have ordinary_kriging_lite_to_geotiff return the field it resolved (e.g. add "value_field": field_name or "__geom_z__" to the returned params dict), write that resolved name into set_archtoolkit_layer_metadata instead of the empty string, and show it once in the completion message, e.g. "Kriging 보간 완료! (값 필드: Elevation)".

#### [minor] DEMGEN-08 — DXF files that fail to load produce no message at all

- 위치: `tools/dem_generator_dialog.py` :719-737
- 문제: An invalid QgsVectorLayer is not an exception, so the `if layer.isValid():` branch simply falls through with no else. When every selected file fails, loaded_count stays 0 and the trailing `if loaded_count > 0:` suppresses the only remaining message, so the button does nothing visible. A partial failure is equally hidden: selecting five files of which two fail reports "3개 DXF 로드 완료" without naming the two that did not load. The success message also reports total_features counted after setSubsetString, so a file that loaded but matched no selected code contributes 0 features with no indication.
- 실패 시나리오: A user selects three .dxf files, one of which is actually an AutoCAD .dwg renamed to .dxf (a common hand-off mistake). OGR cannot open it, layer.isValid() is False, no exception is raised, and the message bar reports "2개 DXF 로드 완료: 총 41,208개 피처". The user assumes all three sheets are in the project, runs DEM 생성, and gets a DEM missing one map sheet's worth of contours — with no record anywhere that a sheet was dropped.
- 제안 수정: Add an else branch to the isValid() check that collects the failed basenames, and after the loop always report both counts, e.g. push_message(..., "경고", f"{loaded_count}개 로드, {len(failed)}개 실패: {', '.join(failed)}", level=1) when failed is non-empty, and an explicit error when loaded_count == 0. Also flag any loaded file whose post-filter featureCount() is 0 so an empty code match is visible at load time rather than at run time.

### ai-context


#### [blocker] AI-CTX-01 — "Nearest site" and the reference-site classification counts are taken from the first 120 features in file order, not the nearest 120

- 위치: `tools/ai_aoi_summary.py` :691-957
- 문제: _reference_sites_summary stops collecting at max_items (default 120, from spinReferenceMax in ai_report_dialog.py:379) and only then sorts `items` by distance_to_aoi_m. The features arrive in provider order from a bbox-filtered QgsFeatureRequest (line 652/666), which is FID/spatial-index order, not distance order. So the 120 kept sites are an arbitrary subset of the sites in the AOI buffer, and the `counts` dict (lines 681-688, 740-756) is likewise tallied only over that subset. ai_local_summarizer.py:286-307 then re-sorts that already-truncated list and asserts `가장 가까운 유적은 X` ("the nearest site is X") as fact, and _reference_sites_lines (ai_local_summarizer.py:144-157) prints the partial counts as the layer's classification. The same truncated `items`/`counts` go verbatim into the Gemini …
- 실패 시나리오: Reference layer = a 유적분포도 GeoPackage with 500 site polygons inside the AOI buffer's bbox, stored in 시군구 order. The site 15 m from the AOI boundary is FID 431. The scan keeps FIDs 1-120 and breaks. Sorting those 120 puts a site 1,850 m away first. The generated report states: `가장 가까운 유적은 \`OO유적\`, AOI 중심 기준 남서쪽, AOI 경계에서 약 1,850 m 거리에 위치합니다.` while a site 15 m from the AOI exists in the very same layer and is never mentioned. The classification line likewise reports `AOI 내부/중첩=3` when the true figure over all 500 is 12. Both numbers go into report.md in the saved bundle (ai_report_dialog.py:125 …
- 제안 수정: Separate the scan cap from the report cap. Inside the loop, compute only the cheap discriminators (distance_to_aoi_m via g.distance, and the within/intersects classification) for every feature up to max_scan, incrementing `counts` for all of them; then sort by distance and compute the expensive per-item area/length splits for the first max_items only. Report `counts` over everything scanned, and add an explicit `scan_truncated` flag (scanned > max_scan) distinct from the existing item-display `t …

#### [blocker] AI-CTX-02 — A Gemini response truncated at maxOutputTokens is presented as a finished report ('완료') and written to report.md

- 위치: `tools/ai_gemini.py` :371-386
- 문제: generate_text parses candidates[0].content.parts and returns the text with error=None, never inspecting candidates[0]["finishReason"]. `grep -rn "finishReason|MAX_TOKENS|usageMetadata" tools/` returns nothing in the whole repository. When Gemini stops because it hit the cap it still returns the partial text together with finishReason "MAX_TOKENS" (same for "RECITATION" and for a mid-stream "SAFETY" stop). ai_report_dialog.py:1064-1069 only treats an empty/whitespace body as a failure, so a partial body flows straight to `self.txtOutput.setPlainText(text)` + `push_message(..., "완료", ...)` ("Complete") at 1087-1089, and _on_export_bundle writes exactly that string to report.md (1258-1262) next to context.json and the CSVs as the deliverable. Aggravating factor: the dialog requests max_output …
- 실패 시나리오: A project with 28 AOI-overlapping result layers produces a context JSON whose per-layer section the model expands into a Korean report. The model spends ~1,500 tokens thinking and emits ~2,500 tokens of report before hitting the 4,096 cap; the API returns parts[0].text ending mid-sentence in section '사용된 레이어/분석 요약' with finishReason MAX_TOKENS. The plugin shows that text, flashes '완료', and the user clicks 번들 저장. report.md in ArchToolkit_AOI_Report_*/ contains a report that silently omits the last 13 layers, the 한계/주의 section and the 다음 단계 제안 section the prompt asked for - and nothing anywhere …
- 제안 수정: Read `fr = candidates[0].get("finishReason")` and return it as a third element (or as a structured result). Treat anything other than STOP as partial: return the text together with a non-None warning, and have ai_report_dialog.py prepend a visible banner (e.g. '※ 응답이 토큰 한도로 잘렸습니다 (finishReason=MAX_TOKENS) - 아래 내용은 불완전합니다.') to both txtOutput and report.md, and not push '완료'. Separately, send generationConfig.thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget = 0 for the 2.5 family so the 4096-token budget is spent o …

#### [major] AI-CTX-03 — '선택된 피처만 사용' with an empty selection silently analyses the entire AOI layer while the report still says only the selection was used

- 위치: `tools/ai_aoi_summary.py` :194
- 문제: In _unary_union_geoms, `selected_only=True` combined with an empty selection falls through to `layer.getFeatures()` - every feature in the layer. The AOI geometry, its area, the buffer and therefore every downstream statistic are then computed over the whole layer, while ctx["options"]["selected_only"] is still written as True (line 1333) and ai_local_summarizer.py:351-354 renders that as the header note `선택 피처만 사용` ("using selected features only"). That is a user-facing string stating something the run did not do. The authors already recognised this exact hazard and handled it correctly one function away, for the reference layer: ai_aoi_summary.py:662-664 has `# Respect "selected only": empty selection means empty result.` and returns an empty feature list. ai_report_dialog.py:848 reads t …
- 실패 시나리오: An AOI layer '2025_시굴조사구역' holds 50 survey polygons spread across a province. The user selects one polygon, ticks '선택된 피처만 사용', then clicks on the map canvas (clearing the selection) before pressing 생성. build_aoi_context unions all 50 polygons; the buffer spans the whole province; ctx.aoi.area_m2 is ~50x the intended area; every layer's feature counts and length/area sums are province-wide. The report prints '- AOI 면적: 41,880,000.0 ㎡' and the header '(선택 피처만 사용)'. The only visible clue is '- AOI 피처 수: 50'.
- 제안 수정: Mirror the reference-layer behaviour: in build_aoi_context, before calling _unary_union_geoms, if `selected_only and aoi_layer.selectedFeatureCount() == 0` return (None, 'AOI 레이어에 선택된 피처가 없습니다. 선택하거나 "선택된 피처만 사용"을 끄세요.'). Alternatively record the effective mode in ctx['options']['selected_only'] (False when the fallback was taken) so the header note cannot lie - but refusing is the safer behaviour, because the fallback silently multiplies the study area.

#### [major] AI-CTX-04 — Per-layer length/area are measured in the layer's own CRS with no geographic guard, so a WGS84 layer yields square degrees in fields named total_area_m2 / total_length_m

- 위치: `tools/ai_aoi_summary.py` :358
- 문제: build_aoi_context enforces a metric CRS for the AOI only (line 1155, is_metric_crs). Scanned layers may be in any CRS, and _vector_layer_stats_in_geom builds its QgsDistanceArea from layer.crs(). QgsDistanceArea.measureArea/measureLength return metres only when an ellipsoid is actually in effect; with the project ellipsoid set to 'NONE' (a normal, selectable QGIS project setting) setEllipsoid('NONE') selects planar measurement, and on a geographic CRS that is degrees and square degrees - returned without raising. The result is stored under the keys total_length_m / total_area_m2, printed by ai_local_summarizer.py:59-61 as '총 길이: X m' and '총 면적: X ㎡', and exported under the CSV headers total_length_m / total_area_m2 (ai_aoi_summary.py:1427-1428, 1487-1488). This exact trap is documented and …
- 실패 시나리오: Project ellipsoid = 'None / Planimetric' (unchanged default in many QGIS setups). AOI in EPSG:5186. A 지적도 polygon layer downloaded in EPSG:4326 overlaps the buffer with one 1 km² parcel block near Seoul (lat 37.5). Planar area in degrees = (1000/88320) x (1000/110996) = 1.132e-2 x 9.009e-3 = 1.020e-4 deg². The report prints '- 총 면적: 0.0 ㎡' and layers_summary.csv records total_area_m2 = 0.000102 for a 1,000,000 ㎡ block. A 5 km EPSG:4326 road layer likewise prints '- 총 길이: 0.1 m' instead of 5,000 m. Both numbers go into the Gemini prompt and into the bundled CSV as metre measurements.
- 제안 수정: Reuse the cadastral module's logic in _safe_distance_area: when QgsProject.instance().ellipsoid() is empty or 'NONE' and the passed CRS is geographic, set 'WGS84' instead and log a warning; then always route results through da.convertAreaMeasurement(a, QgsUnitTypes.AreaSquareMeters) / da.convertLengthMeasurement(l, QgsUnitTypes.DistanceMeters). If an ellipsoidal measurement still cannot be made and the layer units are not metres, omit the key rather than writing a degree value under a *_m2 / *_m …

#### [major] AI-CTX-05 — The 20,000-feature scan cap truncates feature counts and length/area sums, and the context and CSV carry no truncation flag

- 위치: `tools/ai_aoi_summary.py` :395-397
- 문제: After 20,000 features the scan stops, but `features`, `total_length_m`, `total_area_m2` and every entry in `numeric_fields` are emitted as if they were complete. _reference_sites_summary sets an explicit `truncated` flag (line 956) that the local report reads; the layer stats set nothing equivalent. Consequences: (a) the context JSON handed to Gemini (ai_report_dialog.py:718) presents a capped count and a capped sum as totals; (b) layers_summary.csv exports them under the headers `features`, `total_area_m2`, `total_length_m` (lines 1429-1431, 1485-1488) - and that CSV is advertised in the help as '통계 CSV는 AI 없이 AOI 주변 표준 통계를 CSV로 저장합니다'; (c) only the local Markdown report carries a blanket caveat in section 5 ('피처가 매우 많으면(스캔 cap) 일부만 반영되었을 수 있습니다'), which does not say which layer was cappe …
- 실패 시나리오: A cadastral layer with 60,000 parcels intersects a 1,000 m AOI buffer. The scan processes 20,000 and breaks. The context says features: 20000, scanned: 20001, total_area_m2: 18,400,000. The local report prints '- 피처: 20,000 (스캔 20,001)' and '- 총 면적: 18,400,000.0 ㎡'; layers_summary.csv records the same; Gemini writes 'AOI 반경 내 지적 필지는 20,000필지, 총 면적 약 1,840 ha'. The true figures are 60,000 parcels and ~55,200,000 ㎡ - a 3x error in a number that lands in the excavation report.
- 제안 수정: Set out['truncated'] = bool(scanned > int(max_features_scan)) and out['scan_cap'] = int(max_features_scan) in _vector_layer_stats_in_geom; fix the count with `if scanned >= max_features_scan: truncated=True; break` (or decrement before reporting). Add a `truncated` column to the layers CSV fieldnames, have ai_local_summarizer._layer_stats_lines emit a per-layer '(스캔 한도 도달 - 아래 합계는 부분값)' line, and state the cap in the Gemini prompt. Better still, also report layer.featureCount() or a bbox count s …

#### [major] AI-CTX-06 — Raster min/mean/max are computed from a nearest-neighbour subsample with no record that the raster was decimated

- 위치: `tools/ai_aoi_summary.py` :1023-1034
- 문제: When the AOI window exceeds 4 Mpixels the band is read through a smaller buffer, which is GDAL nearest-neighbour decimation - every (scale x scale) block contributes one sample. min and max are therefore sample extrema and are systematically biased inward, and count is a sample count, not the pixel count in the AOI. The returned dict records nothing about this: keys are the bare `count`, `min`, `max`, `mean`. The Gemini prompt receives them as plain numbers, and export_aoi_context_csv writes them under the headers `pixel_count`, `raster_min`, `raster_mean`, `raster_max` (lines 1436-1439, 1496-1499) with no qualifier. Only the local Markdown path hedges, and only partly: ai_local_summarizer.py:111 prints '픽셀(표본) 수' while line 112-114 prints 'min/mean/max' with no sampling note. The downsamp …
- 실패 시나리오: A 1 m LiDAR DSM and an AOI with a 1,500 m radius buffer give a 3,500 x 3,500 = 12.25 Mpixel window. scale = sqrt(12.25/4) = 1.75, so bw = bh = 2000 and roughly one pixel in three is read. The true maximum elevation in the AOI, a single ridge cell at 101.2 m, is not among the samples; the summary reports max = 98.4 m. layers_summary.csv records raster_max = 98.4 and pixel_count = 4,000,000 for an AOI that actually contains 12.25 M pixels, and the Gemini report writes '조사지역 내 표고는 12.3~98.4 m 범위입니다'. Anyone deriving an AOI area from pixel_count x 1 m² is short by a factor of 3.
- 제안 수정: Record the sampling in the stats dict: out['sampled'] = bool(bw*bh < w*h), out['sample_step'] = round(scale, 3), out['window_px'] = w*h. Add matching columns to the layers CSV, print '(표본 1/N)' next to min/mean/max in ai_local_summarizer._layer_stats_lines, and state it in the prompt. For min/max specifically, prefer band.GetStatistics/ComputeStatistics or a blockwise full-resolution pass when the AOI covers most of the raster, since extrema are exactly what decimation destroys.

#### [minor] AI-CTX-07 — gt_0_5_pct is labelled a 'mask/visibility ratio' but its near-binary detector is max-relative while its threshold is a hard-coded 0.5

- 위치: `tools/ai_aoi_summary.py` :1122-1128
- 문제: Two mismatches. (1) The gate accepts any raster whose valid cells are >=98% {0} union {vmax} - that admits a three-valued categorical raster whose middle class is small, and admits a zero-padded continuous raster (a GDAL output written without a NoData value). It is not a binary-viewshed test. (2) Having detected 'binary' relative to vmax, the measurement then thresholds at an absolute 0.5, which is only meaningful when the 'on' value is >=1. ArchToolkit produces a raster that trips case (1) itself: tools/viewshed_dialog.py:2176-2260 writes an Int16 asymmetry raster coded 0 = same, 1 = forward-only, 2 = reverse-only, NoData -9999. The Korean label then calls the result a 마스크/가시 (mask/visibility) ratio, and ai_local_summarizer.py:455-467 ranks rasters by it in section 4 under '0.5 초과 비율이 높은 …
- 실패 시나리오: Case (1): a 가시권 불균등(asymmetry) layer from this plugin where 97% of valid cells are 0, 2% are 1 and 1% are 2. frac = 0.97 + 0.01 = 0.98, so the gate fires; vis = count(>0.5) = 3.0. The report prints '- (힌트) 마스크/가시(>0.5) 비율: 3.0 %' for a raster in which 0% of cells encode visibility - 3% is the fraction where forward and reverse visibility *disagree*. Case (2): a weighted viewshed raster coded {0.0, 0.35} with 60% of the AOI visible. near0 + nearMax = 1.0 so the gate fires, but count(vals > 0.5) = 0, so the report prints '마스크/가시(>0.5) 비율: 0.0 %' for an AOI that is 60% visible.
- 제안 수정: Threshold relative to the detected high value - count(vals > vmax/2.0) - and require exactly two distinct valid values (np.unique on the sample, len == 2) rather than a 98% coverage fraction, so three-class and zero-padded rasters are rejected. Rename the emitted key and the Korean label to a neutral '상위값 셀 비율' unless the layer's archtoolkit metadata (already in item['archtoolkit'], line 1264) identifies it as a viewshed/mask product; only then call it 가시 비율.

#### [minor] AI-CTX-08 — Numeric-field statistics are silently capped at 12 (8 in the local report) and the id-field regex discards single-letter element columns

- 위치: `tools/ai_aoi_summary.py` :84-88
- 문제: Two silent drops in the numeric statistics the report is built on. First, _classify_fields keeps at most 12 numeric fields and _layer_stats_lines prints at most 8 of those, with no '외 N개 필드 생략' note anywhere in the context, the CSV or the Markdown - so a reader cannot tell whether a column was absent from the layer or dropped by a cap. Second, the id-field regex is anchored and case-insensitive, so it discards fields literally named r, g, b, no, rank and index. I verified this directly: the regex matches 'B', 'R', 'G', 'no', 'rank', 'area_no' and rejects 'P', 'K', 'Ca', 'Mg', 'Zn', 'NO3'. The comment at lines 80-83 explains the exclusion as covering terrain-profile RGB colour columns and the cadastral PNU, but the rule is applied to every layer in the project, including a user's own geoche …
- 실패 시나리오: A soil-chemistry point layer from an XRF survey has columns B, P, K, Ca, Fe, Mn, Zn, Cu, Pb, Sr, Rb, Zr, Ti, Ba, V, Cr, Ni, As, Y, Nb (20 numeric fields). The boron column B is dropped by the regex as an RGB colour column. Of the remaining 19, 12 survive the max_numeric cut in the order the fields happen to appear, and the local report prints only 8 of those. The AI report therefore contains means for 8 elements out of 20, and nothing states that 12 were dropped or which ones - a reader who does not see boron concludes it was not measured.
- 제안 수정: Record what was dropped: out['numeric_fields_truncated'] = len(numeric) - len(numeric_ordered) and a '외 N개 수치 필드 생략' line in _layer_stats_lines. Remove r|g|b|rank|no from the generic regex and apply the RGB/PNU exclusion only when the layer's archtoolkit metadata says tool_id is terrain_profile or cadastral_overlap (that metadata is already read at line 1225), so an element column named B on an unrelated layer is kept.

#### [minor] AI-CTX-09 — The 40-layer cap drops layers in layer-id dictionary order, including layers the user explicitly picked, with no per-run record of what was dropped

- 위치: `tools/ai_aoi_summary.py` :1303-1304
- 문제: build_aoi_context stops after max_layers summaries. In the default 'auto' scope the candidate order is QgsProject.mapLayers() key order, i.e. alphabetical by layer id - unrelated to the layer tree, to relevance or to distance from the AOI - so which layers survive is arbitrary. In the 'layers' scope the user picks the layers explicitly through _LayerMultiSelectDialog (ai_report_dialog.py:583-596), which imposes no limit, yet build_aoi_context still silently cuts the list at 40. The context records only options.max_layers: 40; there is no flag saying the cap actually fired and no count of the candidates skipped. ai_local_summarizer.py:385-386 infers it ('레이어 수가 많아 일부만 요약되었을 수 있습니다') from len(layers) >= max_layers, so the local Markdown path is covered, but the Gemini prompt and the bundled …
- 실패 시나리오: After a week of analysis the project holds 58 ArchToolkit result layers overlapping the AOI. The user opens 대상 레이어, ticks all 58, and generates a Gemini report. 40 arbitrary layers (those whose generated ids sort first) are summarised; 18 - possibly including the viewshed and the least-cost path the report was requested for - are absent with no mention. context.json and layers_summary.csv in the saved bundle contain 40 rows and nothing indicating that 58 were requested.
- 제안 수정: Count the candidates that passed the filters and add ctx['layers_truncated'] = True plus ctx['layers_candidates'] = N when the cap fires; surface both in the Gemini prompt and as a note in the CSV or a sidecar. Iterate in layer-tree order (QgsProject.layerTreeRoot().findLayers()) instead of mapLayers() key order so the cut is at least predictable, and in the 'layers' scope either honour the user's full selection or refuse with a message naming the limit.

### covariate-distance-contour


#### [blocker] DIST-01 — Polygons narrower than a cell are silently dropped by the default pixel-centre burn rule, inflating every distance

- 위치: `tools/distance_raster_dialog.py` :385-396
- 문제: QGIS's gdal:rasterize exposes an ALL_TOUCH parameter (verified in release-3_40 rasterize.py line 272: `if self.parameterAsBoolean(parameters, self.ALL_TOUCH, context): arguments.append("-at")`). This call does not pass it, so it defaults to False and GDAL burns a polygon only where a pixel CENTRE falls inside it. Any polygon narrower than one cell burns nothing along most or all of its length. The only guard in the code is the extent check at lines 361-379, which tests bounding-box overlap and therefore cannot see this at all: the features are inside the grid, they just do not survive rasterization. Meanwhile the help at line 496 states "점·선·면 모두 됩니다" (points, lines and polygons all work), which is an unqualified claim that polygons are supported. Lines are genuinely safe (GDAL walks lines …
- 실패 시나리오: A user takes the 하천 (watercourse) AREA layer from a Korean 1:5,000 수치지형도 - perennial channels are typically 3-10 m wide polygons - and a 30 m reference DEM, and builds the classic `distance_water` predictor. Almost no 30 m pixel centre falls inside a 5 m channel polygon, so the burned mask contains cells only at the few wide reaches and confluences. gdal:proximity then measures distance to those isolated cells. A cell sitting 40 m from the channel, which should read ~40 m, reads the distance to the nearest wide reach instead - commonly 800-3000 m. The whole predictor is biased high by an amoun …
- 제안 수정: Either pass "ALL_TOUCH": True for polygon and line sources (the correct semantics for a presence mask feeding a distance transform), or, if the pixel-centre rule is intentional, compare the burned cell count against the source feature count and warn when features were lost - and drop the unqualified "점·선·면 모두 됩니다" claim in favour of a statement that polygons thinner than the reference cell size are not representable on that grid.

#### [blocker] DIST-02 — Nothing checks that the burn produced any target cell, so an all-NoData distance raster is reported as a finished predictor

- 위치: `tools/distance_raster_dialog.py` :397-398
- 문제: The only post-condition tested is that a file exists. The code's own comment at lines 355-360 identifies exactly this risk - "with nothing inside at all, the output would be all NoData and still report success" - but the guard that was written for it (lines 361-379) only tests bounding-box intersection, which is a different condition. GDAL's behaviour is confirmed in alg/gdalproximity.cpp line 384: `if (pafProximity[i] < 0.0f) pafProximity[i] = fNoDataValue;`. When the source band contains no pixel matching VALUES="1", every output pixel stays negative and is therefore written as -9999, the declared NoData. The resulting GeoTIFF opens fine, QgsRasterLayer.isValid() returns True, metadata is stamped, the layer is added to the ArchToolkit group and the message bar says 완료.
- 실패 시나리오: A user selects a 유적 범위 (site-boundary) polygon layer of 12 small excavation footprints, each 15-25 m across, against a 30 m reference DEM, to build `distance_site`. The layer's bounding box covers the DEM, so the overlap guard at line 363 passes silently. No pixel centre falls inside any of the 12 footprints, so the burned Byte mask is uniformly 0 (= its own NoData). gdal:proximity finds no target pixel and writes -9999 into every cell. The user is told "완료: 변수 'distance_site' 생성 (기준 DEM_30m)", sees a layer in the tree, and adds a raster that is 100% NoData to their predictor stack. Downstream …
- 제안 수정: After the rasterize, read the burned band's statistics (e.g. QgsRasterLayer(burned).dataProvider().bandStatistics(1) maximum, or gdal:rasterlayerstatistics) and raise if no cell holds the burn value; after the proximity, do the same on the output and refuse to add a layer whose valid-cell count is zero. Both are cheap compared with the work already done.

#### [blocker] DIST-03 — A non-square reference grid makes every distance wrong: gdal_proximity scales both axes by the X pixel size

- 위치: `tools/distance_raster_dialog.py` :286-293
- 문제: The tool accepts px_x != px_y without comment, passes both to gdal:rasterize ("WIDTH": px_x, "HEIGHT": px_y, line 389-390) so the burned mask inherits the rectangular cell, and then asks gdal:proximity for georeferenced distances. GDAL master alg/gdalproximity.cpp lines 119-127 shows what that does:

    if (std::abs(adfGeoTransform[1]) != std::abs(adfGeoTransform[5]))
        CPLError(CE_Warning, CPLE_AppDefined, "Pixels not square, distances will be inaccurate.");
    dfDistMult = std::abs(adfGeoTransform[1]);

The distance is computed as sqrt(dx^2 + dy^2) in PIXEL INDEX space and then multiplied by a single scalar, the X pixel size, for both axes. GDAL raises only a CPLError warning, which goes to the processing log, not to the message bar or the live log the user is looking at. This co …
- 실패 시나리오: A user's reference raster is a DEM with 10 m x 20 m cells (routinely produced by warping a geographic-CRS grid into EPSG:5179 without forcing square pixels, and accepted by this dialog because only px > 0 is checked). A site 10 cells due north of a river - a true distance of 200 m - is written as sqrt(0^2 + 10^2) * 10 = 100 m, exactly half the real distance. North-south distances are all off by the factor px_y/px_x while east-west distances are correct, so the error is direction-dependent and invisible in a histogram. The layer is named "distance_river (거리, m)" and its metadata records units=" …
- 제안 수정: Refuse a non-square reference raster the way align/export does (compare px_x and px_y within a relative tolerance and push an error naming the two values), or resample the burned mask to a square grid before the proximity call. Silently producing a directionally-scaled distance is the one outcome that must not happen.

#### [blocker] COV-01 — Cancelling the sampling silently truncates the study area and still produces a full correlation/VIF report

- 위치: `tools/covariate_report_dialog.py` :334-368
- 문제: `break` abandons the scan and returns whatever rows were gathered, and _on_run (lines 302-310) cannot distinguish a truncated sample from a complete one: its only test is `matrix.shape[0] < 10`. Above that threshold it computes the correlation matrix and VIFs and renders the report as if nothing happened. Crucially the scan is ordered - `ys` ascends from extent.yMinimum() (line 323) - so a cancellation does not thin the sample, it geographically clips it to a southern band of the common extent. The report header (line 379) prints "유효 표본 {n:,}점" with no cancellation flag, the log line at 309 records no flag, and the CSV export carries none either. The project's own changelog in metadata.txt lists "viewshed gains ... cancel honesty" as a fixed defect, so this is a known class of bug that thi …
- 실패 시나리오: A user runs the report over a 30 km x 30 km common extent with 5 covariates and the default 3,000 points, which produces ny=55 rows. The scan is slow (5 provider.sample() calls per point), so after about half a minute they press 취소 at row 27. 1,485 points have been collected, every one of them from the southern half of the sheet. The dialog does not close or warn - it goes straight to the report window showing "변수 5개 · 유효 표본 1,485점" with a correlation matrix and colour-coded VIF verdicts. The user saves the CSV and reports r = 0.74 between slope and TPI for their study area, when the number de …
- 제안 수정: Track the cancellation (`cancelled = True` before the break) and return it alongside the matrix. In _on_run, either abort with "취소되었습니다 — 리포트를 만들지 않았습니다", or, if a partial report is wanted, stamp the truncated y-range and the cancelled flag into the report header, the log line and the CSV so it cannot be mistaken for a full-extent result.

#### [major] DIST-04 — A user-typed variable name can make a continuous distance raster be classified as categorical downstream

- 위치: `tools/distance_raster_dialog.py` :428-441
- 문제: `key` is `distance_<whatever the user typed>` (tools/predictor_naming.py:169-182), and it is written straight into the `kind` metadata field. tools/raster_semantics.py:29-31 matches `kind` by SUBSTRING against CATEGORICAL_KIND_HINTS = ("class", "category", "categor", "litho", "geolog", "slope_position"). So any distance variable whose name contains one of those fragments is classified as a raster of nominal class codes. Two consumers act on that with no further check: tools/align_export_dialog.py:708/723 resamples it with nearest neighbour instead of bilinear, and lines 994-997 write its key into the ready-to-paste CATEGORICAL_PREDICTORS list handed to the downstream model. tools/covariate_report_dialog.py:227-233 also labels it "(범주형 — 상관/VIF 부적합)" and unchecks it. The tool even walks the …
- 실패 시나리오: A user builds the standard "distance to a geological contact" predictor from a KIGAM contact line layer named "Geology_boundary". The dialog auto-suggests "geology_boundary", the hint label confirms the output variable will be `distance_geology_boundary`, and the run produces a Float32 raster of metres from 0 to ~40,000. Because "geolog" is a substring of the kind, align/export then resamples those metre values with nearest neighbour and lists `distance_geology_boundary` in CATEGORICAL_PREDICTORS. The model is told a continuous distance in metres is a nominal factor with tens of thousands of l …
- 제안 수정: Distance output is always continuous, so record that fact explicitly rather than letting a substring rule infer it - e.g. write a `continuous: true` (or `semantic: "distance"`) key in the metadata params and have is_categorical_meta honour an explicit declaration before falling back to the substring hints. Failing that, prefix-exclude any kind starting with "distance_" in tools/raster_semantics.py.

#### [major] COV-02 — "모두 선택" checks categorical rasters, and a categorical raster without ArchToolkit metadata is never detected at all - Pearson r over class codes is then reported as an ordinary correlation

- 위치: `tools/covariate_report_dialog.py` :242-247
- 문제: The categorical exclusion is only a default check-state (line 233) plus a label suffix (line 230). _check_all ignores the exclusion entirely, unlike its sibling _check_arch_only (lines 248-252) which does honour the Qt.UserRole+1 flag. Worse, the detection depends on ArchToolkit metadata: tools/utils.py:437-445 returns {} for any layer without an archtoolkit/tool_id or run_id custom property, so a categorical GeoTIFF the user loaded from disk - a land-cover grid, a soil-class grid, a lithology raster from outside the plugin - yields is_categorical=False, gets no label, and is indistinguishable from a continuous variable. Nothing re-checks at run time: _on_run (line 270) just takes whatever is checked. And once the report is built, the categorical marking is gone completely - _build_report_ …
- 실패 시나리오: A project holds slope, TPI, distance_water and a KIGAM lithology raster (tool_id kigam_raster, correctly labelled "(범주형 — 상관/VIF 부적합)" in the list). The user clicks the 모두 선택 button - the obvious action - and runs. Lithology is included with no further warning. The report prints a row "geology_class | VIF 3.21 | 양호" in green and r = +0.41 against slope, rendered identically to the real correlations. Those numbers are Pearson products over arbitrary KIGAM integer class codes: re-numbering the legend changes them. The user saves the CSV, which also has no categorical column, and reports "litholo …
- 제안 수정: Make _check_all skip items flagged categorical (it already has the flag in Qt.UserRole+1), re-check the selection in _on_run and refuse or warn loudly when a categorical layer is included, and carry a 범주형 marker into both the report table and the CSV so the exported artefact is self-describing. For rasters with no ArchToolkit metadata, at least fall back on the band type plus a small distinct-value probe, or give the list a per-item "범주형으로 취급" toggle so the user can declare it.

#### [major] COV-03 — Listwise deletion is applied but never quantified: one high-NoData variable silently shrinks and geographically restricts the sample for every variable

- 위치: `tools/covariate_report_dialog.py` :343-368
- 문제: A grid point is kept only if every selected raster returns a valid value. That is a defensible method and the help at line 474 does say "모든 변수가 유효한 점만 사용합니다" - but nothing anywhere records HOW MUCH was lost or WHICH variable lost it. The report prints only the surviving count (line 379: "변수 {k}개 · 유효 표본 {n:,}점"); the number of grid points attempted (nx*ny), the retention rate, and the per-variable valid counts are computed nowhere and shown nowhere. So a variable that drops 0% of the grid and one that drops 94% of it produce reports that look identical. The CSV export (lines 441-457) does not even carry n. This matters most for the plugin's own outputs: a distance raster built with MAX_DISTANCE set is NoData everywhere beyond the cap (verified in alg/gdalproximity.cpp line 384), and a cumu …
- 실패 시나리오: A user has five predictors over a 20 km x 20 km sheet: elevation, slope, TPI, distance_water (full coverage) and distance_site built earlier with 최대 거리 = 2000 m, so it is NoData outside a 2 km halo of the known sites. The shared 3,025-point grid is intersected with that halo: roughly 170 points survive, all of them clustered around known sites. The report says "변수 5개 · 유효 표본 170점" and prints r = 0.81 between slope and TPI, flagged as redundant. That correlation describes the near-site halo - the very sub-population whose terrain signature the model is trying to isolate - not the sheet. The use …
- 제안 수정: Count attempted points and per-variable valid counts during the scan and put them in the report and the CSV: attempted n, retained n, retention %, and a per-variable "valid at N of M points" column. Warn on the message bar when retention falls below, say, 50%, naming the variable with the largest NoData footprint. Also refuse (or loudly flag) a run where n is not comfortably larger than k, since the VIF is otherwise meaningless.

#### [major] COV-04 — The sampling grid ignores the raster cell size, so the reported "유효 표본 N점" can exceed the number of distinct cells sampled

- 위치: `tools/covariate_report_dialog.py` :319-323
- 문제: The lattice spacing is derived only from the extent and the requested point count; the rasters' pixel sizes are never consulted. When the spacing falls below the cell size, several lattice points land in the same cell and provider.sample() returns the identical value for each, so the same observation is entered into the correlation matrix multiple times. The report then states that count as the sample size (line 379: "유효 표본 {n:,}점"), which is the number a user copies into a paper. The same line is also the only place the sample is characterised, and it says nothing about the points being a regular lattice over spatially autocorrelated surfaces - so nothing anywhere signals that the effective number of independent observations is far below n. (The module docstring at line 23 compounds the c …
- 실패 시나리오: A user restricts the report to a 1 km x 1 km AOI on a 30 m DEM stack. The AOI holds 33 x 33 = 1,089 cells. With the default 3,000 points the code computes ny = round(sqrt(3000)) = 55 and nx = round(3000/55) = 55, i.e. 3,025 lattice points at 18.2 m spacing - below the 30 m cell size, so on average 2.8 points fall in each cell. The report prints "변수 5개 · 유효 표본 3,025점" and the user writes "correlations were computed on n = 3,025 sample points" in their methods section. At most 1,089 distinct cells exist, and after spatial autocorrelation at 30 m the effective n is a small fraction of that. A rev …
- 제안 수정: Cap the lattice density at the coarsest selected raster's cell size (compute the spacing, compare it to max(rasterUnitsPerPixelX) across the selected layers, and reduce nx/ny when it is finer), tell the user the effective point count that resulted, and add one sentence to the report stating that the points are a regular lattice over autocorrelated surfaces so n is not an effective sample size for any significance test. Also fix the docstring at line 23 to say "grid" rather than "random".

#### [major] CONT-01 — Contour interval is in the DEM's vertical unit but the layer name, the completion message and the machine-readable metadata all assert metres

- 위치: `tools/contour_extractor_dialog.py` :291-305
- 문제: gdal_contour's -i is expressed in whatever unit the DEM band holds; the DEM's vertical unit is never read or checked. The help at line 100 admits this honestly ("등고선 간격은 DEM의 높이 단위(보통 m)를 기준으로 합니다"), but four other places state metres as fact: the UI label "등고선 간격 (m):" (contour_extractor_dialog_base.ui line 174), the temp filename, the layer name 등고선_{interval}m, the completion message, and - the one that cannot be caught by eye - the layer metadata units="m" and params.interval_m, which is a machine-readable claim that other ArchToolkit code and any manifest consumer will take at face value. A disclosure buried in the help dialog does not cure a false unit written into metadata.
- 실패 시나리오: A user loads a DEM supplied in feet (common for imported or converted terrain products; QGIS displays it without complaint) and enters 10. gdal_contour produces contours every 10 FEET, i.e. every 3.05 m. The layer arrives named "등고선_10m", the message bar says "등고선 생성 완료 (간격: 10m)", and the metadata records units="m", interval_m=10.0. The user draws a site plan whose contour legend reads "등고선 간격 10 m" and computes a slope or a relief figure from it; every vertical measurement taken off that plan is out by a factor of 3.28, and the layer metadata will propagate the false unit to anything that re …
- 제안 수정: Read the DEM's vertical unit where it is available (QgsRasterLayer.crs().verticalCrs() / the band unit string) and, when it is not metres or not known, either refuse or stamp the actual unit into the layer name, the message and the metadata instead of hard-coding "m". At minimum make the UI label read "등고선 간격 (DEM 높이 단위)" and write units to match what was actually used.

#### [minor] COV-05 — The AOI restricts sampling to the bounding rectangle only, and neither the label nor the help says so

- 위치: `tools/covariate_report_dialog.py` :280-290
- 문제: resolve_aoi_extent returns geom.boundingBox() (tools/aoi_extent.py:186), so the AOI polygon's shape is discarded and only its envelope is used. The control is labelled "AOI 제한(선택):" with no tooltip, and the help HTML (lines 463-477) does not mention the AOI at all. The rest of this codebase is careful to say 범위 when it means the rectangle - tools/ahp_suitability_dialog.py:355 labels the equivalent control "AOI 범위로 자르기(권장)" - so a user reasonably reads the unqualified "AOI 제한" here as a restriction to the polygon.
- 실패 시나리오: A survey area is an L-shaped 조사구역 polygon following a valley floor; its bounding rectangle is roughly three times its area and takes in the ridge slopes on both sides. The user selects it as the AOI expecting correlations for the surveyed valley. The lattice is generated over the rectangle, so roughly two thirds of the sample sits on ridges that were never surveyed - terrain where slope and TPI covary very differently from the valley floor. The reported r and VIF describe the rectangle, and neither the dialog, the report, nor the CSV says that the polygon boundary was not applied.
- 제안 수정: Relabel the control to match the rest of the plugin (e.g. "AOI 범위(사각)로 제한(선택):"), add a tooltip stating that only the bounding rectangle of the AOI is used, and record the AOI layer name plus the rectangle in the report header. If true polygon restriction is wanted, test each lattice point against the unioned geometry before sampling.

#### [minor] COV-06 — Correlation-matrix headers are truncated to 10/14 characters, so two variables can appear under identical labels

- 위치: `tools/covariate_report_dialog.py` :398-401
- 문제: Column headers are cut to 10 characters and row headers to 14, with no ellipsis, no tooltip and no legend mapping the abbreviations back to full names. The VIF table immediately above uses full names (line 393), so the two tables in the same report label the same variables differently. Distance-raster layers are exactly the case that collides, because they all share the `distance_` prefix (9 characters) by construction.
- 실패 시나리오: A user builds `distance_river_main` and `distance_river_trib` with the Distance tool, plus slope and TPI, and runs the report. Every column header for both distance variables renders as "distance_r" and every row header for both renders as "distance_river" - two identical labels in each axis. The matrix shows r = -0.12 in one cell and r = +0.83 in another and the reader cannot tell which pair each belongs to. The screenshot goes into the report as evidence that the two hydrography variables are not redundant, while it is in fact unreadable.
- 제안 수정: Number the variables (V1..Vk) in both the row and column headers and print a V-number-to-full-name legend under the matrix, or keep the full names with a title attribute and let the table scroll. Any scheme is fine as long as two distinct variables cannot render as the same string.

#### [minor] CONT-02 — A contour run that produces zero lines is reported as 완료 and adds an empty layer

- 위치: `tools/contour_extractor_dialog.py` :290-306
- 문제: The success test is file existence plus layer validity. gdal_contour creates the GPKG table and the ELEV field even when no contour level falls inside the DEM's elevation range, so the layer is valid with zero features and passes both tests. featureCount() is never consulted. The dialog then accept()s and closes, so the user is left with an empty layer in the tree and a green 완료 message.
- 실패 시나리오: A user works on a low-relief excavation area whose DEM spans 12.3 m to 19.8 m and, following a report template, enters an interval of 100 m. No multiple of 100 lies in [12.3, 19.8], so gdal_contour writes no features. The dialog reports "등고선 생성 완료 (간격: 100m)", closes, and adds 등고선_100m to the project. Nothing on screen distinguishes this from a run that failed to reach the DEM at all, and if the layer is not switched on immediately the user carries an empty contour layer into their map composition.
- 제안 수정: Check output_layer.featureCount() before reporting success and, when it is zero, say so with the DEM's actual min/max elevation and the interval that was used, so the user can see the interval is larger than the relief - and keep the dialog open rather than accept()ing.

#### [minor] CONT-03 — No way to declare a NoData value, and passing layer.source() discards any NoData the user set on the QGIS layer

- 위치: `tools/contour_extractor_dialog.py` :274-287
- 문제: IGNORE_NODATA=False plus NODATA=None means gdal_contour uses only the NoData value DECLARED IN THE FILE (confirmed in QGIS release-3_40 contour.py: `if nodata is not None: arguments.append(f"-snodata {nodata}")`). The comment claims this protects against -9999/-32768 fill, but it only does so when the file itself declares that fill as NoData. A NoData value the user configured in QGIS Layer Properties is stored on the layer, not in the file, and passing layer.source() hands GDAL a bare path that cannot carry it. The dialog exposes no NoData field, so there is no way for the user to supply one, and nothing warns that the DEM has no declared NoData.
- 실패 시나리오: A user loads a 국토지리정보원 DEM converted from ASCII grid whose -9999 fill was never written into the GeoTIFF's NoData tag. QGIS renders it as a black block until they set "추가 NoData 값 = -9999" in Layer Properties, after which the DEM looks correct. They run 등고선 추출 at 10 m. gdal_contour, reading the file directly, sees -9999 as real terrain and draws roughly a thousand contour rings between -9990 and 0 around the fill area, on top of the real contours. The message bar says "등고선 생성 완료 (간격: 10m)" and the layer is added.
- 제안 수정: Read the layer's effective NoData (dataProvider().sourceNoDataValue(1) and any userNoDataValues) and pass it as the NODATA parameter when one exists; when the DEM has no NoData at all, warn before running and offer a NoData field in the dialog so the user can supply the fill value.

#### [minor] REF-01 — REFERENCES.md promises a source for every algorithm the plugin calls, but the Distance tool (gdal:proximity) and the Correlation/VIF report have no entry

- 위치: `REFERENCES.md` :1-16
- 문제: The document states an explicit contract and the section list (verified by `grep -n '^## '`) has no heading for either tool. tools/distance_raster_dialog.py:385 and :419 call gdal:rasterize and gdal:proximity via processing.run, which the legend defines as category (A). tools/covariate_report_dialog.py:100-123 implements VIF = 1/(1-R^2) in Python, which the legend defines as category (B), and the report presents the |r| >= 0.7 and VIF >= 5 / >= 10 cut-offs to the user as verdicts ("제거 권장", "주의") with no attribution - even though the same document takes care elsewhere to flag which grade breaks are the plugin's own display convention rather than a published classification.
- 실패 시나리오: A user writing a methods section cites REFERENCES.md as the plugin's source list, as it invites them to. They find no entry for the two variables their model leans on hardest - distance to water and the multicollinearity screen - and either omit the method citation entirely or cite the plugin itself. A reviewer asks where the VIF >= 10 removal rule and the |r| >= 0.7 threshold come from, and the plugin's own reference document, which claims to cover every algorithm it uses, has no answer.
- 제안 수정: Add an (A) section for the Distance tool citing the GDAL gdal_proximity/gdal_rasterize documentation, and a (B) section for the Correlation/VIF report citing the VIF definition and the thresholds actually applied (e.g. Dormann et al. 2013 for |r| > 0.7 in predictor screening, and the conventional VIF 5/10 rules of thumb), noting per the document's own convention which cut-offs are the plugin's display choice. NOTE: REFERENCES.md is one of the 19 files under concurrent remediation, so re-check it …

### styling-core


#### [blocker] STYLE-01 — Re-running map styling silently erases any user layer parked in the style group from the Layers panel

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :413-453 (rescue at 424-429, destruction at 450-453)
- 문제: _teardown_style_group only re-parents layers found inside a sub-group whose name matches the literal string "원본 레이어 (숨김)". Every other layer node still inside the style group is destroyed by root.removeChildNode(group) at line 451. Layers tagged tool_id="map_styling" are deliberately deregistered (fine, they are this tool's own outputs), but a *user's own* layer that sits anywhere else in the group is neither deregistered nor rescued: its only QgsLayerTreeLayer node dies with the group while the layer stays registered in QgsProject. A registered layer with no layer-tree node is not drawn and does not appear in the Layers panel, and QGIS offers no undo for layer-tree operations. The docstring at 414-421 shows the author knew about exactly this failure mode, but the guard covers one hard-cod …
- 실패 시나리오: User runs Map Styling once; the tool creates the group "Style: 도면 데이터" and files their DXF layers under it. Tidying up, the user drags their own "유적분포도" point layer into "Style: 도면 데이터" (a natural place, since the tool already put their data there), or simply renames the sub-group "원본 레이어 (숨김)" to "원본" with F2. They run Map Styling again. created_any is True, so line 373 calls _teardown_style_group: findGroup("원본 레이어 (숨김)") returns None (renamed) or the dragged layer is not inside it, the tool_id filter does not match a user layer, and line 451 removes the whole group. "유적분포도" - and, in the re …
- 제안 수정: In _teardown_style_group, walk group.findLayers() once: re-parent to root every layer whose metadata tool_id is NOT "map_styling" (regardless of which sub-group it sits in), and only then deregister the map_styling-tagged ones and remove the group. Do not key the rescue on a translatable group name; if a name is needed, mark the sub-group with a custom property (e.g. group.setCustomProperty("archtoolkit/role", "sources")) and look that up instead.

#### [major] STYLE-02 — Applying styling silently relocates the user's source layers into a hidden group and resets their visibility, contradicting the shipped changelog

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :379-392
- 문제: Every selected layer is torn out of wherever the user had it in the layer tree and re-added under a new, switched-off group. Because a brand new QgsLayerTreeLayer is constructed, the node's own state is not carried over: QgsLayerTreeLayer(layer) is checked by default, so a layer the user had deliberately switched OFF comes back switched ON, and its position, its parent group and its expanded/legend state are lost. Nothing in the dialog discloses this: the group box reads "1. 스타일을 적용할 원본 레이어 선택" (select the source layers the style will be applied to), the help HTML at 142-150 mentions only classification/aggregation and styling, and README.md and the help text never mention a hidden group. metadata.txt:22, which the QGIS Plugin Manager shows to the user, states the opposite outright: "map s …
- 실패 시나리오: User has a tidy project: group "기초자료" containing 수치지형도 DXF layers, with a 500k-feature cadastral layer deliberately unchecked because it is slow to draw. They tick those layers in Map Styling and press 스타일 적용. The layers vanish from "기초자료" and reappear inside "Style: 도면 데이터 > 원본 레이어 (숨김)", all re-created as checked. They run the tool a second time: _teardown_style_group re-inserts each of them at root index 0 as a fresh (checked) node, so the 500k-feature cadastral layer is now switched ON at the top of the tree, redrawing on every pan and covering the styled map, and the "기초자료" group is empty …
- 제안 수정: Either (a) stop moving the user's layers: leave the source nodes where they are and only add the new styled group, or (b) make it opt-in with a checkbox ("원본 레이어를 숨김 그룹으로 이동") that is off by default, and preserve node state when moving (copy itemVisibilityChecked/isExpanded from the old node onto the new one, or use clone() of the existing node). Also correct the metadata.txt claim, which currently says user layers are no longer mutated.

#### [major] STYLE-03 — Building polygons are always re-buffered by 0.01 map units, inflating every footprint (≈1.1 km in a geographic CRS)

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :610-636 (buffer at 630)
- 문제: poly_geom is only ever computed inside the `geom.type() == LineGeometry` branch. Any input that is already a polygon (a shapefile of 건물 footprints, or a DXF whose closed polylines were imported as polygons) therefore falls through to the else branch and is passed through QgsGeometry.buffer(0.01, 2) — a positive 0.01-map-unit outward buffer with only 2 segments per quarter circle — instead of being used as-is. The buffer distance is in layer map units and there is no CRS check anywhere in this module, although seven other modules in the plugin guard with utils.is_metric_crs. Nothing in the UI or the help text says building geometry is buffered at all.
- 실패 시나리오: (a) Metric CRS (EPSG:5186): a 4.0 m × 4.0 m 주거지 footprint (16.00 m²) is silently replaced by its 1 cm outward buffer with chamfered corners — 16.16 m², +1.0 % — and every footprint on the published 도면 is 2 cm too wide. Any area computed from the "Style: 건물" layer is wrong and nothing records the offset. (b) Geographic CRS (EPSG:4326, which DXF/shapefile imports frequently end up in): 0.01 degrees ≈ 1.1 km, so each building becomes a ~2.2 km wide blob and the buildings layer is unusable, again with a "시각화 완료" success message.
- 제안 수정: Take the direct path when the input is already a polygon: `if geom.type() == QgsWkbTypes.PolygonGeometry: poly_geom = QgsGeometry(geom)`. Keep the buffer strictly as a last-resort rescue for point/degenerate input, derive its distance from the layer CRS (refuse or warn on a geographic CRS via utils.is_metric_crs, as the cost/viewshed/trench tools do), and state the buffer distance in the dialog. Also check the pr.addFeatures() return at line 646: a feature whose geometry stayed a line is rejecte …

#### [major] STYLE-04 — Multi-part building outlines are concatenated into one ring, producing self-intersecting footprints

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :613-621 (ring built at 618)
- 문제: For a multi-part line feature every part's vertices are flattened into a single list and handed to fromPolygonXY as ONE exterior ring. Separate parts (an outer wall and an inner courtyard, or two wings of the same building block) are therefore joined by a straight segment that jumps from the end of one part to the start of the next, producing a self-intersecting (bowtie) polygon. fromPolygonXY does not validate, and the guard on line 625 only rejects null/empty geometry, so the malformed polygon is accepted. The single-part branch has the same weakness: a polyline with fewer than three distinct vertices yields a degenerate, non-null polygon that is also accepted.
- 실패 시나리오: A 수치지형도 DXF building entity imported as one MultiLineString with two parts — outer ring [(0,0),(10,0),(10,10),(0,10),(0,0)] and courtyard ring [(3,3),(7,3),(7,7),(3,7),(3,3)] — produces ring = the 10 vertices of both parts in sequence. The resulting polygon zig-zags from (0,0) out to the courtyard and back, so its rendered footprint and its computed area (roughly 100 − 16 = 84 m² expected, but an arbitrary self-intersecting value in practice) are both wrong, and the 도면 printed for the excavation report shows a building shape that does not exist on the ground.
- 제안 수정: Build one ring per part and let the first/largest part be the exterior with the rest as interior rings (QgsGeometry.fromPolygonXY([ring0, ring1, ...])), or use QgsGeometry.polygonize()/forceRHR on the collected lines. Validate the result (isGeosValid(), non-zero area, >= 4 distinct vertices) before accepting it, and count and report the features whose polygonization failed instead of silently passing them to the buffer fallback.

#### [major] STYLE-05 — A missing or malformed map_styling_codes.json silently falls back to built-in defaults while the dialog still shows the file path

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :101-102 with 161-176 and 236-241
- 문제: _load_code_config records why it fell back in self._code_config_load_error, but the constructor never surfaces it — only reload_code_config() (line 256-257), which runs solely when the user clicks 다시 불러오기. _sync_code_config_ui unconditionally writes the JSON path into lblCodeConfigPath, so the dialog asserts that this file is the mapping in force even when the file is missing, unreadable, or not a JSON object and the built-in defaults are being used instead. Both the help HTML (line 147) and README.md line 168 actively invite users to edit that file, which is exactly how a syntax error gets introduced. The per-rule validation has the same property: a rule whose width is missing/non-numeric (line 202-209) or whose code is not a JSON string (199-200) is dropped with only a log line.
- 실패 시나리오: User follows the help text, opens tools/map_styling_codes.json, adds their own 철도 codes to "roads" and leaves a trailing comma. json.load raises, _code_config_load_error is set, the constructor ignores it, and lblCodeConfigPath still shows .../tools/map_styling_codes.json. They press 스타일 적용, get "시각화 완료", and produce a 도면 rendered from the eight built-in road codes — their added codes are absent and the widths they tuned were never applied. Nothing in the UI ever mentions the fallback.
- 제안 수정: Surface the error at construction time: if self._code_config_load_error is set, push_message at warning level and set lblCodeConfigPath to something like "(기본 매핑 사용 중) <path> — <error>" with a distinct colour, and keep the warning visible until a successful reload. Additionally report the number of rules dropped by the per-rule validation instead of only logging it.

#### [major] STYLE-06 — DEM grayscale layer is rendered with no contrast enhancement, so it is not stretched to the DEM's value range

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :493-497 and 830-836
- 문제: QgsSingleBandGrayRenderer constructed this way has no QgsContrastEnhancement attached (QGIS only attaches one through QgsRasterLayer.setDefaultContrastEnhancement / the style UI, never through setRenderer). The raw band value is therefore used directly as the 0-255 grey level, with no stretch to the band's minimum/maximum — note that the colour layer immediately below, at lines 517-531, does call dataProvider().bandStatistics() to get min/max, so the intent to span the DEM's real range is clear and only the grey layer is missing it. The same unstretched renderer is written into the exported dem_gray.qml at line 832, so the defect propagates to every project that reuses the preset.
- 실패 시나리오: A Korean 5 m DEM with elevations from 12 m to 1,180 m: every pixel above 255 m is mapped to the same extreme grey level (clamped, or wrapped modulo 256 depending on the QGIS build), so the "..._그레이" layer carries no elevation information over ~95 % of the map and either contributes nothing under the 40 % Multiply blend or paints a hard artificial boundary along the 255 m contour that a reader of the report will take for a real terrain break. The UI ("배경 지형 스타일링 적용 (음영기복 + 고도채색)") and README.md line 167 ("DEM 배경에 hillshade, grayscale, color 스타일 적용") both present this grey layer as an elevation …
- 제안 수정: Attach a stretch built from the same statistics already read for the colour layer: create a QgsContrastEnhancement(dataProvider().dataType(1)), setContrastEnhancementAlgorithm(QgsContrastEnhancement.StretchToMinimumMaximum), setMinimumValue(min_val)/setMaximumValue(max_val), and pass it to the renderer's setContrastEnhancement() — in both style_dem_background and export_qml_preset.

#### [major] STYLE-07 — Exported roads/rivers/buildings QML hard-code the field name "Layer" and have no ELSE rule, so re-applying a preset can render nothing

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :790-815 (template field at 793), rule expression at 687, field candidates at 551
- 문제: The preset templates are always built against a field literally named "Layer", while the tool's own detect_code_field (line 551) accepts five different names: 'Layer', 'layer', 'RefName', 'LayerName', 'LAYER'. The root rule is created with no symbol and no ELSE child, so a feature that matches none of the code rules is simply not drawn. Applying an exported .qml to any layer whose code field is not spelled "Layer" produces a renderer whose every rule filter references a non-existent field: all features fall through to a non-existent ELSE and the layer renders completely empty, with no error dialog. The UI hint ("선택한 스타일(도로/하천/건물) QML과 현재 DXF 코드 매핑 JSON을 폴더로 저장합니다"), the help HTML ("QML/프리셋 내보내기로 프로젝트 재사용성을 높일 수 있습니다") and README.md line 187 ("프로젝트 간 재사용에 유리합니다") all promise cross-project r …
- 실패 시나리오: User exports a preset from project A (whose DXF layer has the field "Layer"), opens project B where the same 수치지형도 data was loaded as a shapefile with the code field "RefName", right-clicks the layer → Properties → Style → Load Style → roads.qml. QGIS loads the rule-based renderer without complaint; every rule filter reads "Layer" = 'A0023211', the field does not exist, nothing matches, and there is no ELSE rule, so the road layer draws as a blank map. The user has no indication that the preset did not apply.
- 제안 수정: Name the field the preset targets explicitly in lblPresetHint and in the manifest ("이 QML은 코드 필드 이름이 'Layer'인 레이어에만 적용됩니다"), or generate one QML per supported field name, or build the rule filters from an expression that tolerates the alternatives. Independently, give the root rule a catch-all ELSE child with a neutral symbol so unmatched features are visibly drawn rather than silently dropped — the same fix also makes unmapped DXF codes visible instead of invisible.

#### [major] STYLE-08 — "프리셋을 저장했습니다" is shown even when every file failed to write

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :772-892 (message at 892)
- 문제: Every write in export_qml_preset is individually guarded: _save_named_style returns False on failure (760-770) and each json.dump is wrapped in try/except with log_swallowed. The `exported` list is accumulated but never consulted. The final push_message reports success at level=0 (green) unconditionally, so a run in which zero files were produced is indistinguishable from a complete export. The user-facing text "프리셋을 저장했습니다" (the preset has been saved) is then simply untrue.
- 실패 시나리오: User picks a read-only or full destination (a mounted network share, a directory owned by another user, or a full disk). os.makedirs(preset_dir, exist_ok=False) raises, preset_dir silently becomes base_dir (line 781-783), every saveNamedStyle returns ok=False and both json.dump calls raise OSError — all swallowed to the log file. The message bar shows a green "프리셋을 저장했습니다: /mnt/share" and the dialog stays usable. The user closes QGIS believing the preset exists; the folder contains nothing. (The same message also hides the fallback: when makedirs fails for any other reason, the files are writt …
- 제안 수정: Report on `exported`: if it is empty, push at level=2 with the reason (collect the failures rather than only logging them); if it is partial, push at level=1 listing which files were written and which failed; only push the green message when everything succeeded. Also surface the preset_dir fallback (line 781-783) as a warning instead of reusing the user's folder silently.

#### [minor] STYLE-09 — Layers with an unrecognised code field, and features whose codes are absent from the JSON, are dropped with no count and no warning

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :574-576 and 589-591, message at 402
- 문제: Two silent filters operate on the user's data. (1) A selected layer whose attribute table has none of the five candidate names ('Layer', 'layer', 'RefName', 'LayerName', 'LAYER') is skipped by `continue` with no log and no message. (2) Only features whose code is literally in the JSON list are collected; every other code in the DXF is dropped. Neither the number of layers skipped, nor the number of features matched, nor the set of codes present in the data but absent from the mapping is ever reported, yet the completion message calls the result a 통합 (integrated) layer, and a code injected into the filter is not escaped, so a code containing an apostrophe makes the whole expression invalid and yields zero features for that category — again silently.
- 실패 시나리오: User ticks ten layers: six 수치지형도 DXF layers (field "Layer") and four layers converted to shapefile by a colleague, whose code column is "UFID". detect_code_field returns None for the four shapefiles, they are skipped, and the roads layer contains only the DXF sheets. The message bar says "시각화 완료 … 도로, 하천, 건물". The user prints the 도면 for the report with roughly 40 % of the road network missing and no indication that anything was left out.
- 제안 수정: Accumulate per-layer statistics in aggregate_features (layers skipped for lack of a code field; features matched per category; distinct codes seen in the source but not present in the mapping) and report them — a warning-level message bar entry plus a line in the layer's archtoolkit/params_json. Escape single quotes when building the IN list, and check QgsExpression validity before using the filter.

#### [minor] STYLE-10 — DEM styling applies a z-factor-1 hillshade to any DEM, including a geographic-CRS one, with no CRS guard

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :477 and 824
- 문제: QgsHillshadeRenderer computes slope from elevation differences divided by the pixel size expressed in the layer's map units, with a default z-factor of 1. When the DEM is in a geographic CRS the pixel size is in degrees while the elevations are in metres, so the computed gradients are wrong by a factor of roughly 1e5 and the shading saturates. This module performs no CRS check at all, although tools/utils.py provides is_metric_crs precisely for this and seven other modules (cost_surface, cost_network, viewshed, trench_suggestion, kriging_lite, spatial_network, ai_aoi_summary) refuse or warn on a geographic CRS. The 0.1.4 changelog even advertises geographic-CRS guards being added elsewhere.
- 실패 시나리오: User loads an SRTM tile in EPSG:4326 (elevation in metres, pixel ≈ 0.000277°), selects it as the 배경 지형 DEM and ticks 배경 지형 스타일링. dz/dx is ~1e5 times too large everywhere, so the hillshade renders as a near-uniform black/white surface with no readable relief, and the "..._음영기복" layer tagged units="m" goes into the report as a terrain product. No warning is shown.
- 제안 수정: Before styling the DEM, check utils.is_metric_crs(dem_layer.crs()); on a geographic CRS either refuse with an explanatory message (as cost_surface does at 2143) or set the renderer's z-factor to an approximate degrees-to-metres correction and state in the layer name/metadata that an approximation was applied.

#### [minor] STYLE-11 — unload() never closes the live-log singleton, leaving a plugin window behind and duplicating it on reload

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/arch_toolkit.py` :342-437 (pump stopped at 350-353); tools/live_log_dialog.py:291-292
