- 문제: ArchToolkitLiveLogDialog is a module-level singleton parented to iface.mainWindow(), and ensure_live_log_dialog is called by four dialogs (dem_generator, cadastral_overlap, cost_surface, viewshed). unload() stops the log pump and tears down the three persistent tool dialogs, the toolbar, the menu and every QAction, but never touches _live_log_dialog, and the dialog has no WA_DeleteOnClose. Because it is parented to the main window its C++ object survives unload; because QGIS drops the plugin's modules on unload, the module global holding it is discarded, so the next load constructs a second dialog. The old one also keeps an event filter installed on whichever tool dialog was last passed to attach_owner (live_log_dialog.py:236-239), which is never removed.
- 실패 시나리오: User opens 비용표면, which pops the "ArchToolkit 작업 로그" window, then uses Plugin Reloader (or unticks/reticks the plugin in the Plugin Manager). The first log window stays on screen but is now orphaned — the new module's listener set is empty, so it never updates again. Opening any tool creates a second window with the identical title; the user now has two apparently identical log windows, only one of which is live.
- 제안 수정: Export a close/reset helper from live_log_dialog (remove_ui_log_listener, attach_owner(None), close(), deleteLater(), _live_log_dialog = None) and call it from ArchToolkit.unload() right after stop_ui_log_pump(); set Qt.WA_DeleteOnClose or clear the singleton in closeEvent so a closed window is not resurrected as a stale object.

#### [minor] STYLE-12 — The plugin log file has no size cap or rotation, and the swallow-logger can write two lines per feature

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/utils.py` :93-127 and 289-307; amplification at tools/map_styling_dialog.py:604-606
- 문제: The in-memory queue is bounded (_UI_LOG_QUEUE_MAX = 5000) and the live-log widget is bounded (setMaximumBlockCount(5000)), but the file sink is not: archtoolkit.log under the QGIS profile directory is appended to forever with no rotation, size check or truncation, and every one of the ~380 log_swallowed call sites added in 0.1.3 writes to it. _write_log_line re-opens and closes the file for every single line. Call sites inside per-feature loops amplify this: the reprojection handler in aggregate_features logs the same exception twice for every feature it skips.
- 실패 시나리오: A DXF sheet with 200,000 matching features is aggregated while the coordinate transform fails per feature: 400,000 lines (~50 MB) are appended with 400,000 separate open/write/close cycles, stalling the styling run and growing a log file the user is never told about. Over months of normal use the same file grows without bound, because nothing ever trims it.
- 제안 수정: Give _write_log_line a size check (e.g. rotate to archtoolkit.log.1 above ~5 MB, keep one or two generations) or hold one open handle with a bounded writer; de-duplicate the doubled log_swallowed call at map_styling_dialog.py:604-606 and rate-limit or aggregate swallow-logging inside per-feature loops (log the first occurrence plus a final count).

#### [minor] STYLE-13 — Styling outputs are temporary scratch layers, which the tool never discloses

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/map_styling_dialog.py` :566-567 and 402
- 문제: Every vector product of this tool is a memory (scratch) layer whose contents are not written anywhere; the DEM products are clones that reference the original raster. The completion message says a 통합 레이어 was created, the UI header promises 고고학 도면 표준 visualisation, and README.md line 166 describes the tool as bundling DXF layers into styled products — none of them says the result is discarded when the project closes. This matters more here than for other tools because this tool also moves the user's own source layers into a switched-off group (see STYLE-02): after a round trip the project can end up with no visible map data at all.
- 실패 시나리오: User styles their 수치지형도, saves the project (dismissing QGIS's generic temporary-layer warning), and reopens it a week later to print the 도면. The three "Style: …" layers are gone, and their original DXF layers are still sitting unchecked inside "Style: 도면 데이터 > 원본 레이어 (숨김)", so the project opens with an empty canvas and the user has to re-run everything.
- 제안 수정: State it in the dialog (a hint label next to 스타일 적용) and in the completion message, e.g. "결과는 임시 스크래치 레이어입니다 — 프로젝트를 닫으면 사라집니다", and/or offer to write the aggregated layers to GeoPackage in a user-chosen folder, the way the other output-producing tools in this plugin do.

### shared-core


#### [blocker] SC-01 — tri_radius reports artificially smooth terrain next to a NoData boundary, as a real value, for the exact reason it NaNs the grid rim

- 위치: `tools/terrain_math.py` :122-132 (docstring claim at 67-70)
- 문제: The rim of the grid is NaN'd because a truncated window "would be computed from a truncated neighbourhood and read as artificially smooth terrain" (line 127-128). A NoData boundary truncates the window in exactly the same way, but is not NaN'd: only `counts == 0` is. A cell with 15 valid neighbours out of 48 gets `sqrt(sq_total/15)` and is written out as an ordinary float, indistinguishable from a full-window value. The docstring (lines 67-70) states the NaN rule as "Cells whose window holds no valid neighbour come back as NaN, as do the outermost radius rows and columns", so the truncation at a NoData edge is not disclosed either. This is not an exotic configuration: a DEM clipped to the survey polygon (the normal output of Clip raster by mask layer) is NoData everywhere outside the AOI, …
- 실패 시나리오: I built a 41x41 DEM: a flat plateau (z=0) for columns 0-20 and a scarp dropping 40 m per cell for columns 21+, then set nodata_mask=True for columns 21+ - i.e. the DEM was clipped at the lip of the gorge, which is what happens when the survey-area polygon ends at the scarp. tri_radius(z, radius=3) on the unclipped grid gives 57.15 m at the lip cell (row 20, col 20). tri_radius(z, radius=3, nodata_mask=mask) on the clipped grid gives **0.00 m** at the same cell - every valid neighbour is on the flat plateau side - and 0.00 m at cols 18 and 19 too (true values 15.28 m and 34.16 m). It is not NaN …
- 제안 수정: Apply the rim rule to every truncated window, not just the grid rim: after the accumulation, `result[counts < (2 * radius + 1) ** 2 - 1] = np.nan` (this subsumes the counts==0 and the four rim slices). If partial windows are wanted deliberately, return `counts` alongside `result` so the caller can mask or at least publish the neighbour count, and change the docstring at lines 67-70 to say that a NoData boundary truncates the window the same way the grid edge does.

#### [major] SC-02 — PER_CELL_BYTES is total process RSS divided by N, not marginal per-cell memory, so cost_budget over-predicts memory ~7x and refuses runs that fit - with no way to override

- 위치: `tools/cost_budget.py` :12-24 (the measured table), 42-45 (the constant), 57-58, 220-225
- 문제: The table divides *total process peak RSS* by the cell count. That is why the quoted figure falls from 240 to 168 to 128 B/cell as N grows - the ~30 MB Python+NumPy baseline is being amortised, exactly as the following sentence says ("Per-cell cost falls as fixed overhead amortizes") - and then the constant is set to the 1M-cell figure rounded UP to 160, which bakes that fixed baseline into a per-cell rate. The true marginal cost is what the accumulation allocates: dist float64 (8) + prev int32 (4) + dem float32 (4) + nodata_mask bool (1) + friction float32 (4) + a live heap that is empirically negligible = ~21.5 B/cell. So PER_CELL_BYTES over-predicts by roughly 7x, and since `cells > budget` is tested BEFORE `ALWAYS_ALLOW_CELLS` and before `confirmed`, the resulting refusal is one the us …
- 실패 시나리오: I transcribed _dijkstra_full (cost_surface_dialog.py:792-874) verbatim, drove it with the real tools/cost_models.edge_cost (Tobler) plus a friction plane, and measured VmHWM against a baseline taken after the dem/mask/friction allocations: 1,000,000 cells -> 12.3 MB delta (12.9 B/cell) random, 12.0 MB (12.6 B/cell) smooth; 4,000,000 cells -> 46.0 MB (12.1 B/cell) random, 46.7 MB (12.3 B/cell) smooth. Peak live heap was 6,688 entries at 1M cells (0.007/cell), so the heap is not 110 B/cell. Concretely: a laptop whose QGIS session reports MemAvailable = 4 GB gets cell_budget = 13,421,772. A 5 m D …
- 제안 수정: Set PER_CELL_BYTES from the marginal allocation rather than from total RSS - 8 (dist) + 4 (prev) + 4 (dem) + 1 (mask) + 4 (friction) = 21, doubled to 48 for headroom is still 3x measured - and replace the table with delta-RSS figures (baseline subtracted) so the docstring stops presenting an amortised interpreter footprint as a per-cell rate. Independently, move the `cells <= ALWAYS_ALLOW_CELLS` short-circuit above the `cells > budget` refusal so the constant means what its comment says.

#### [major] SC-03 — The runtime estimate shown before a cost-surface run covers one accumulation, but the worker runs up to four

- 위치: `tools/cost_surface_dialog.py` :2426 and 2449-2459 (estimate); 1210-1217 and 1224-1270 (what actually runs); tools/cost_budget.py:125-126, 197-232 (the contract)
- 문제: assess()/estimate_seconds() are defined for ONE full-grid accumulation of `cells` cells - cost_budget's own module docstring describes "a Dijkstra accumulation ... one pop and eight edge relaxations per cell", singular, and assess_batch exists precisely because "Time is cumulative" for repeated runs. The cost-surface dialog calls the single-run assess() and prints its `minutes` verbatim as "예상 소요 시간", but the worker then runs one _dijkstra_full per entry in `stages` (up to three: time surface, energy surface, corridor-from-end) plus an A* pass for the path. The number in the confirmation dialog is the one the user uses to decide whether to start the job, and nothing in the dialog says it covers only the first stage. cost_network_dialog.py:470-495 gets this right by using assess_windows ove …
- 실패 시나리오: User ticks 시간 래스터 + 에너지 래스터 + 회랑(corridor) and picks a window of 8,000,000 cells. _confirm_analysis_size computes verdict.minutes = 8,000,000 * 1.05e-5 / 60 = 1.4 min and, since 1.4 < 1.5, prints "예상 소요 시간: 약 84초". At the module's own constant that is 4.2 minutes; at the 18 us/cell I measured on the real loop it is 7.2 minutes, i.e. the user was told 84 seconds and waits five times longer with only the task-manager progress bar t …
- 제안 수정: Compute the stage list in _confirm_analysis_size the same way the worker does (it already knows create_time_raster / create_energy_raster / create_corridor / create_path) and call cost_budget.assess_batch(cells, n_stages, ...) - which exists for exactly this and returns the batch total in `seconds` - instead of assess_current_machine(cells). If the stage count is not available there, at minimum label the figure as per-stage in the dialog text. Re-check against the current tree: this file is on t …

#### [minor] SC-04 — resolve_aoi_extent reprojects only the AOI's vertices, so a few-vertex AOI box from a geographic CRS silently loses up to ~460 m off its southern edge with status OK

- 위치: `tools/aoi_extent.py` :168-185
- 문제: QgsGeometry.transform() moves the existing vertices and nothing else; the straight segments between them are re-drawn as straight segments in the target CRS, but their true images are curved. boundingBox() therefore returns the hull of the transformed VERTICES, which for a polygon whose extreme point lies mid-edge is strictly inside the true extent of the user's AOI. The module was written specifically so that "falling back to the full extent silently is how a clipped export quietly becomes an unclipped one" (lines 70-74) - but the reverse error, an extent quietly SMALLER than the AOI, is returned as STATUS_OK with skipped=0, so message() returns "" and every caller (align_export_dialog.py:605-619, covariate_report_dialog.py:282-297, ahp_suitability_dialog.py:1225-1243 and 1749-1764) proce …
- 실패 시나리오: A user has an AOI drawn as a 4-vertex rectangle in EPSG:4326 (a KML/GPS box is the normal source) spanning 126.0-128.0 E, 36.0-37.0 N, and a DEM in EPSG:5186 (TM, central meridian 127 E). Along the southern edge (lat 36.0) the northing in EPSG:5186 is minimal at lon 127.0 and rises toward the corners. I computed this with a full Krueger series on GRS80: N(36.0, 126.0) = 4,585,547.30 m at the corner versus N(36.0, 127.0) = 4,585,542.67 m at the midpoint for a 0.1-degree half-width, rising to a 462.5 m difference at a 1.0-degree half-width. So for this AOI the returned rect.yMinimum() is 462 m n …
- 제안 수정: Take the bounding box first and let QGIS densify it: `rect = transform.transformBoundingBox(geom.boundingBox())` when the CRSs differ, keeping the existing usability/empty checks on the result. Equivalent alternative: densify the union geometry before transforming.

#### [minor] SC-05 — zt_curvature names Zevenbergen & Thorne (1987) but returns the negation of both equations they publish, and the one caller's provenance note misattributes the convention to ESRI

- 위치: `tools/terrain_math.py` :15 (docstring), 41-42
- 문제: Zevenbergen & Thorne (1987) publish profile curvature as -2(DG^2 + EH^2 + FGH)/(G^2 + H^2) and plan curvature as +2(DH^2 + EG^2 - FGH)/(G^2 + H^2). This code has the leading signs the other way round on both, which is ArcGIS's convention, not ZT's. The coefficients D, E, F, G, H themselves are exactly ZT's and are correct (I verified them analytically at cell = 1, 5 and 30 m), so this is purely the published sign convention, but the docstring at line 15 says "Zevenbergen & Thorne (1987) profile/plan curvature" with no note, and terrain_math is the shared, QGIS-free module a future caller will read. The sole current caller does disclose the convention correctly in the layer name (terrain_analysis_dialog.py:1111-1116, "부호규약: 음=볼록" / "음=수렴" - both of which I confirmed against the code), so th …
- 실패 시나리오: Verified by running zt_curvature on analytic surfaces. For z = -x^2 (an upwardly convex slope) the code returns profile = -2.0 at every cell size tested; the Zevenbergen & Thorne equation as published returns +2.0. For z = -x - y^2 (a ridge running east-west, i.e. sidewardly convex) the code returns plan = +2.0; ZT returns -2.0. Concrete harm path: a reviewer asked to check a 곡률-종단 layer opens the layer metadata, reads "this is the NEGATION of the Z&T formula as printed by ESRI", re-runs the same DEM through ArcGIS Pro's Curvature (Profile) tool, gets values with the SAME sign as the plugin's, …
- 제안 수정: In terrain_math.py, state the convention where the author is named: "returns the ArcGIS sign convention (negative profile = upwardly convex), which is the negation of the equations printed in Zevenbergen & Thorne (1987) and of GRASS r.slope.aspect / SAGA". Then correct the metadata string in terrain_analysis_dialog.py:1129-1133 to say the values agree with ESRI/ArcGIS and are the negation of the ZT paper and of GRASS/SAGA. Do not change the numbers - the layer names already document the shipped …

#### [minor] SC-06 — Zero-weight edges are silently discarded by both Dijkstra paths, so a node can report degree 1 and closeness 0.0 in the same output row

- 위치: `tools/network_metrics.py` :42-43 and 100-101
- 문제: An edge whose weight is exactly 0 is dropped rather than traversed at cost 0, in dijkstra_weighted and again in betweenness_centrality_weighted, with nothing written anywhere - no log_swallowed, no counter, no note in the module docstring, which only says "Weighted variants (Dijkstra) drive the cost network". Dropping a non-finite or negative weight is defensible (Dijkstra has no answer for a negative edge); dropping a zero-cost edge is not - the shortest-path answer for it is well defined. The caller applies the identical filter before building the adjacency (cost_network_dialog.py:2443-2444) but keeps the same edge in `pairs` (line 2408), which feeds degree (line 2412) and the Union-Find component assignment (line 2414). The two halves of one output feature therefore disagree with each o …
- 실패 시나리오: Two site points of the same 유적 fall in the same cell of a 5 m DEM (or are 3 m apart), so the accumulated cost between them is exactly 0.0 and edge_weights[(a,b)] = 0.0. cost_network_dialog.py:2411-2414 counts that edge: node a gets degree 1 and is unioned into a component of size >= 2. cost_network_dialog.py:2443 then skips it, so adj[a] is empty. If it was a's only edge, closeness_centrality_weighted returns 0.0 for a (dijkstra returns all-inf, `reachable` is empty, line 64-66) and betweenness returns 0.0. The exported node table then carries, on one row, degree=1, comp_size=2, closeness=0.0 …
- 제안 수정: Treat a zero weight as a real zero-cost edge (change the guard to `ww < 0` plus the isfinite check) - Dijkstra handles zero-weight edges correctly and Brandes' tie handling already covers the equal-distance case. If zero must remain excluded, call swallow_log.log_swallowed (already imported at line 17) or return a dropped-edge count, and make cost_network_dialog exclude the same edge from `pairs` so degree/component and centrality agree. cost_network_dialog.py is on the concurrent remediation li …

#### [blocker] CRS-01 — Curvature is computed with the AVERAGE of the two pixel sizes, but the kernel it is handed to documents that argument as the SQUARE cell size - a rectangular DEM yields curvature off by tens of percent, published as 1/m

- 위치: `tools/terrain_analysis_dialog.py` :1102 (also 983)
- 문제: zt_curvature takes ONE cell size and uses it for both the x and the y second differences (L2 = cell*cell in both D and E, and cell in both G and H). Its docstring states the argument is the square cell size. The caller passes the arithmetic mean of |gt[1]| and |gt[5]| with no check that they are equal and no warning when they are not, so on a rectangular grid D is scaled by (dx/cell)^2 and E by (dy/cell)^2. The layer is then published as "곡률-종단 profile (Z&T 1987 …)" with units="1/m" and cell_size recorded as the mean, so nothing downstream can detect it. I executed the kernel directly to bound the class: on a …
- 제안 수정: Read dx = abs(gt[1]) and dy = abs(gt[5]) separately. #### [blocker] CRS-03 — is_metric_crs accepts EPSG:3857, so every tool whose only CRS check is that helper silently reports Web Mercator grid distances as ground metres - 26% long, 59% over on area, at Korean latitudes

- 위치: `tools/utils.py` :324-329
- 문제: The helper only asks whether the CRS is projected with metre map units. EPSG:3857 (Web Mercator) answers yes to both and is an extremely common working CRS the moment an XYZ/OSM basemap or a web-served layer is added, but its point scale factor is 1/cos(phi) - 1.2601 at 37.5N. Every tool in the list above then does planar arithmetic in grid metres and labels the result in ground metres. The failure is worse than silence: viewshed_dialog.py:1307-1316 pushes an explicit error naming the unit when the check fails, so a pass reads to the user as "the CRS was checked and accepted for distance work". Nothing in tools/, README.md or docs/ mentions 3857 anywhere (verified by grep). The contrast inside the plugin is instructive: terrain_profile_dialog.py:1073-1124 does NOT use is_metric_crs - it no …
- 실패 시나리오: A DEM reprojected to EPSG:3857 to sit on an OSM basemap, a hillfort at 37.5N. (1) Viewshed: 최대 거리 = 1000 m. gdal_viewshed -md is in the DEM's georeferenced units, so the analysis radius on the ground is 1000 x cos(37.5) = 793 m; the output layer is named 가시권_단일점_1000m, the ring layer is named 관측반경_1000m, and the metadata records max_dist_m 1000.0. - 제안 수정: Tighten the helper rather than every caller: in is_metric_crs, after the existing test, compute the point scale distortion at the data's latitude (QgsCoordinateReferenceSystem + QgsDistanceArea: measure one known-length line in the CRS ellipsoidally and compare with its planar length) and return a third state, or add a companion is_true_scale_crs(crs, extent) that callers use to push a non-blocking warning naming EPSG:3857 and recommending a Korea 2000 belt (EPSG:5186/5187). #### [major] CRS-04 — Grave-avoidance fetch rectangle is grown by a metre value in the topographic layer's own CRS, unlike the two sibling helpers in the same file that convert to degrees - a geographic topo layer inflates the grave count reported to the user and written into the layer metadata

When that layer is geographic, max(5.0, grave_buffer_m + 3.0) is applied as at least 5 DEGREES. Every feature returned is then run through _feature_has_grave_hint, buffered by grave_buffer_m and unioned, with no test that it is anywhere near the AOI - so grave_count, which the tool pushes to the message bar as '(무덤 회피: N건 반영)' and writes into the published layer's metadata as grave_matched_features, counts graves from a region hundreds of kilometres wide. The fetch rectangle is the AOI bbox grown by max(5.0, 6.0) = 6 DEGREES - roughly 660 km N-S and 530 km E-W at 37.5N, i.e. the entire Korean peninsula. Every 묘지/분묘 feature in the sheet set passes the rectangle filter; suppose 12,438 of them match the grave keyword/code test. Each is buffered by 3 m and unaryUnion'd (a national-scale union on the GUI thread, itself a hang risk), and the run finishes with '트렌치 후보 8개를 생성했습니다. (무덤 회피: 12438건 반영)' while the published trench laye …
- 제안 수정: Apply the identical conversion the same file already uses at :703-708: compute grow = max(5.0, float(grave_buffer_m) + 3.0) and, when topo_layer.crs().isGeographic(), divide by 111320.0 * max(0.2, cos(radians(min(85, |bb.center().y()|)))). While there, count only the graves that actually reach the AOI (intersect the AOI geometry grown by grave_buffer_m) so grave_matched_features means what the message bar claims it means.

#### [minor] CRS-05 — Distance raster rejects a geographic CRS but accepts any other projected CRS, while the spinbox suffix, the layer name, the metadata and the help all state the output is in metres

- 위치: `tools/distance_raster_dialog.py` :254-261
- 문제: isGeographic() is a weaker test than the is_metric_crs() helper this codebase already has and uses in seven other tools. A projected CRS whose map units are feet, US survey feet or links passes the check, and gdal:proximity with UNITS=0 (georeferenced) then returns distances in those units. The help text at :508 states the rule the code implements ('투영 CRS여야 거리 단위가 미터가 됩니다') as if being projected were sufficient for metres, which is exactly the false implication; the layer name and the machine-readable units="m" make it unrecoverable downstream, since align/export and the covariate/AI report read that metadata. The same isGeographic-only pattern is used in terrain_analysis_dialog.py:356, slope_aspect_drafting_dialog.py:232, trench_suggestion_dialog.py:958, geochem_polygonize_dialog.py:1343 …
- 실패 시나리오: A reference raster in a projected CRS with foot map units (e.g. an imported grid carrying EPSG:2264-family metadata, or a DEM whose .prj was written with LINEAR UNIT US survey foot). ref_crs.isGeographic() is False, so the run proceeds. The user sets 최대 거리 = 500 with the ' m' suffix showing; gdal:proximity clamps at 500 FEET = 152.4 m, every cell beyond that becomes NoData, and the surviving values are foot distances. The published predictor is named 'distance_river (거리, m)', its metadata records units="m" and max_distance_m=500, and a logistic model trained on it reports a coefficient per met …
- 제안 수정: Replace the isGeographic() test with the existing utils.is_metric_crs() and name the offending unit in the message the way viewshed_dialog.py:1307-1316 does (QgsUnitTypes.toString(ref_crs.mapUnits())). Correct the help bullet at :508 to say a metre-unit projected CRS is required, not merely a projected one. Consider the same substitution in the five other isGeographic-only call sites listed above.

#### [minor] CRS-06 — Polygon-pick tolerance is computed in canvas map units and then applied as an offset around a point already transformed into the layer's CRS, combined with setLimit(10) - clicking a polygon in a differently-unit'd CRS silently falls back to point mode

- 위치: `tools/viewshed_dialog.py` :396-425
- 문제: tol is in canvas map units but is applied to a coordinate that has already been reprojected into the layer's CRS. The identical operation in terrain_profile_dialog.py:1499-1503 is written in the correct order - grow the rectangle in canvas units first, then ct_inv.transformBoundingBox(rect) into the layer CRS - so the right pattern exists in the codebase. Here, with a metric canvas and a geographic polygon layer, tol becomes degrees, and the setLimit(10) then truncates the result to an arbitrary ten features out of a rectangle hundreds of kilometres across. On a miss the caller at :1195-1226 does not report anything: `hit` is None, control falls through to the single-point branch at :1228, the label changes to '선택된 위치: x, y' and the user gets a point-based reverse viewshed instead of the p …
- 실패 시나리오: Project/canvas CRS EPSG:5186, zoomed so mapUnitsPerPixel = 2.0 m, so tol = 10.0. The 유적분포 polygon layer is in EPSG:4326. The user selects 역방향 가시권, clicks inside a site polygon at (127.05, 37.48). pt_layer is correct, but rect spans 127.05±10 by 37.48±10 degrees - roughly 2,200 km - so setFilterRect returns the whole layer and setLimit(10) keeps ten features in provider order. For a layer of ~250 사적 polygons the clicked one is in that first ten about 4% of the time; the other 96% of clicks produce hit = None, and the dialog silently switches to a single-point observer at the clicked location wi …
- 제안 수정: Use the order terrain_profile_dialog.py:1499-1503 already uses: build the rectangle around canvas_point in canvas units, then transform it into the layer CRS with QgsCoordinateTransform(canvas_crs, layer.crs(), QgsProject.instance()).transformBoundingBox(rect) before setFilterRect. Drop or greatly raise setLimit(10) (the containment test that follows is the real filter), and when the loop finds nothing while the user was in polygon-target mode, say so with a push_message instead of silently degr …

### sweep-processing-params


#### [major] PP-1 — Weiss landform classification tags the wrong NoData value, so the whole NoData collar is mapped and legended as "깊은 곡저 (Incised Valley)"

- 위치: `tools/terrain_analysis_dialog.py` :850-887
- 문제: The call omits NO_DATA. gdal_calc.py then picks the output NoData itself: `if NoDataValue is None and not hideNoData: myOutNDV = DefaultNDVLookup[myOutType]` (gdal_calc.py:437-439), and DefaultNDVLookup[gdal.GDT_Int16] = -32768 (gdal_calc.py:53). gdal_calc then OVERWRITES the plugin's carefully masked zeros: `myResult = ((1*(myNDVs==0))*myResult) + (myOutNDV*myNDVs)` (gdal_calc.py:631), where myNDVs is 1 wherever ANY input equals its own NoData. So every collar cell ends up holding -32768, not 0, and the band NoData is written as -32768 (gdal_calc.py:448-449). The plugin then calls `layer.dataProvider().setNoDataValue(1, 0)`, and QgsGdalProvider::setNoDataValue (qgsgdalprovider.cpp:4049-4078) calls GDALSetRasterNoDataValue(band, 0) — it REPLACES the file's -32768 with 0 and sets mSrcNoData …
- 실패 시나리오: Take a 5 m DEM clipped to a watershed or 행정구역 polygon, so ~40% of the raster's bounding box is DEM NoData (-9999). gdaldem writes -9999 for slope and TPI over that collar (gdaldem_lib.cpp:3901 etc.), and also for the 1-px border because COMPUTE_EDGES is left at the toolbox default False. Run 지형 분석 with 지형분류 (Weiss) ticked, thresholds at the defaults (경사 5°, TPI ±1.0). gdal_calc writes -32768 across the collar; setNoDataValue(1, 0) makes -32768 valid; the resulting layer "지형분류 (TPI 3x3, 경사:5°, TPI:-1.0-1.0)" renders that entire 40% of the map in #08306b under the legend entry "1 | 깊은 곡저 (Incise …
- 제안 수정: Pass `'NO_DATA': 0` in the gdal:rastercalculator call so gdal_calc's propagation writes the same 0 the mask_expr already produces and flags 0 on the band — then the existing setNoDataValue(1, 0) is consistent rather than destructive. (Equivalently, drop the setNoDataValue(1, 0) call and let the -32768 GDAL wrote stand; but the explicit NO_DATA is safer because it also pins the value the metadata implies.) Re-check against the current tree first — this file is in the concurrent remediation set.

#### [major] PP-2 — Distance-to-features rasterize omits -at, so sub-pixel polygons are dropped and an all-NoData distance raster is reported as a successful run

- 위치: `tools/distance_raster_dialog.py` :385-397
- 문제: ALL_TOUCH is not passed, so gdal_rasterize runs without `-at` (rasterize.py:208-209) and burns polygons by the pixel-centre rule: a polygon that contains no pixel centre burns nothing. The layer picker accepts polygon layers (lines 117-120 set PointLayer|LineLayer|PolygonLayer). The pre-flight guard the code added for exactly this class of problem only tests bounding-box overlap — `prepared_extent.intersects(extent)` at line 363 and `extent.contains(prepared_extent)` at line 368 — so a feature that intersects the grid but falls between pixel centres passes both checks and is dropped without a word. The post-run check is only `os.path.exists` (line 398 and line 420), and gdal_proximity with no target pixels writes the nodata value everywhere, so an entirely empty result is indistinguishable …
- 실패 시나리오: Reference raster: a 5 m DEM on EPSG:5186. Target layer: 수치지형도 하천 polygons, where a stream channel is a 3 m-wide polygon running diagonally across the AOI, plus a 4 m x 4 m 저수지 polygon. Partial case: along the diagonal stretches where no 5 m pixel centre falls inside the 3 m channel, nothing burns, so "distance to water" for cells beside that stretch is measured to the next stretch that did burn — a site 4 m from the stream is reported at, say, 85 m, and that number goes into the predictor stack and the report. Total case: if the pond is the only feature selected (선택한 피처만 on) and its 4 m square …
- 제안 수정: Pass `"EXTRA": "-at"` in the gdal:rasterize call (the ALL_TOUCH parameter is not registered in QGIS 3.40, so EXTRA is the only route), and after the burn open `burned` and check that at least one cell equals 1 — raise the same loud RuntimeError the code already raises for a non-overlapping extent when nothing burned. If -at is deliberately not wanted for area-faithful burning, say so in the tooltip and still add the empty-burn check.

#### [major] PP-3 — Viewshed silently substitutes the unclipped raster when the circular clip produces no file, changing the reported 가시비율 under an unchanged layer name

- 위치: `tools/viewshed_dialog.py` :2004-2016 (and the identical block at 2160-2174)
- 문제: QGIS's GDAL Processing algorithms shell out to gdalwarp and return normally whether or not the subprocess succeeded — this plugin knows that, which is why tools/gdal_outcome.py and tests/test_gdal_outcome.py exist and why align_export_dialog.py:858 wraps its warp in _GdalOutcomeFeedback and fails closed on a missing success marker. Here the same failure mode is handled by copying the UNCLIPPED viewshed into final_output and carrying on. Nothing downstream can tell the two apart: the layer keeps the name 가시권_단일점_{max_dist}m and the metadata block (kind="viewshed_single", max_dist_m=...) is written identically. The two rasters are not interchangeable: after a successful clip, cells outside the circle are -9999 and flagged NoData; in the fallback they are gdal_viewshed's out-of-range value 0, …
- 실패 시나리오: AOI polygon 2 km x 2 km, observer at its centre on a 5 m DEM, 최대거리 500 m, and suppose everything within 500 m is visible. Normal run: tot_px = cells of the AOI inside the 500 m circle = 785,398 m² / 25 m² = 31,416; vis_px = 31,416; the message bar reports "가시비율 100.0% | 가시면적 785,398 m² / 785,398 m²". If gdalwarp fails without raising on that one run (a cutline OGR export hiccup, a full temp volume, a GDAL cutline error), the fallback copies the unclipped raster: tot_px now = every AOI cell = 4,000,000 / 25 = 160,000, vis_px is still 31,416, and the same dialog reports "가시비율 19.6% | 가시면적 785,39 …
- 제안 수정: Do not substitute. Either route this clip through the existing GdalOutcomeTracker/_GdalOutcomeFeedback path so a non-success gdalwarp raises, or — if the fallback must stay — name the layer and the metadata differently (e.g. 가시권_단일점_{max_dist}m (원형 마스크 실패, 미클립)) and push a level=1 message, and skip or annotate the AOI statistics for that layer, since its NoData semantics no longer match what the percentage assumes. Re-check against the current tree first — this file is in the concurrent remediat …

#### [minor] PP-4 — Cumulative/reverse viewshed warp passes DATA_TYPE 5, which is Int32 in gdal:warpreproject, not the Float32 the sibling calls document

- 위치: `tools/viewshed_dialog.py` :2616 and 4042
- 문제: This is the exact enum trap the sweep was commissioned to find, caught in the act. gdal:warpreproject's list is prefixed with 'Use Input Layer Data Type' (warp.py:76: ['Use Input Layer Data Type', 'Byte', 'Int16', 'UInt16', 'UInt32', 'Int32', 'Float32', ...]), so index 5 is Int32 and Float32 is 6. The two cliprasterbymasklayer calls in the same file (lines 2009 and 2164) pass 6 with the comment `# Float32`, and terrain_analysis_dialog.py:607/661 and ahp_suitability_dialog.py:1953 also use 6 for Float32 in warpreproject, so the intent at these two sites is plainly Float32 and the code gets Int32. Both sites are uncommented, which is why the mismatch survived.
- 실패 시나리오: No output value is corrupted today, and I checked rather than assumed: the source is gdal_viewshed's 0/255 Byte raster, RESAMPLING is 0 (nearest) so nothing is averaged, -dstnodata -9999 is representable in Int32, and combine_viewsheds_numpy (line 3660-3700) casts to float32 and tests `vs_data > 0.5` and `vs_data != vs_nodata`, both of which behave identically for Int32 input. The failure is latent and one edit away: these are the warps feeding the weighted cumulative viewshed (`val_to_add = float(w)` at line 3676) and the normalised 0-100% mode (line 3702-3707). The moment anyone moves the we …
- 제안 수정: Change both to `"DATA_TYPE": 6,  # Float32` and add the same trailing comment the sibling calls carry, so the next reader can check the integer against the list without opening warp.py. Re-check against the current tree first — this file is in the concurrent remediation set.

### sweep-nodata


#### [major] ND-01 — Cumulative/union viewshed detects each input's NoData and then throws it away: DEM NoData inside the analysis circle is written as a real 0 = "보이지 않음", and the AOI visibility percentage divides by it

- 문제: combine_viewsheds_numpy starts from `cumulative = np.zeros((target_height, target_width), dtype=np.float32)` (3642). Per input point it builds `vis_mask` and then explicitly removes NoData from it — `if vs_nodata is not None: vis_mask &= (vs_data[:h_overlap, :w_overlap] != vs_nodata)` (3700-3701) — so the code *knows* which cells carry no data. That knowledge is then discarded: the only NoData written to the output is the area outside the union of the observer circles, `cumulative[~circular_mask] = nodata_value` (3719-3720). Every cell inside the circle union that had no DEM keeps the initial 0.0 and is written as valid data on a band whose NoData is -9999 (3728). Downstream, apply_count_style labels 0 as "보이지 않음" (4334) and _compute_aoi_visibility_stats_layer treats it as analysed data: ` …
- 실패 시나리오: A DEM clipped to a survey-area polygon (routine in Korean fieldwork: the .tif is rectangular, the data inside it is not). Result the user reads: the map legend colours the collar "보이지 않음" (a cl …
- 제안 수정: Accumulate a validity mask alongside `cumulative` in the per-point loop (e.g. `covered |= (vs_data[:h,:w] != vs_nodata)` when vs_nodata is not None, else `covered[:h,:w] = True`), and at step 5 write `cumulative[(~circular_mask) | (~covered)] = nodata_value` instead of masking on circular_mask alone. If that mask cannot be derived from the per-point rasters, read the DEM's own NoData once onto the target grid and use it. Either way the AOI statistic then measures visible area over *analysable* a …

#### [major] ND-02 — Line-of-sight silently drops NoData samples, so the observer/target elevations are taken from whatever cell happened to be valid — the visibility verdict can invert and the segment lengths are off by the gap

- 위치: `tools/viewshed_dialog.py` :3174-3186, 3202-3203, 3271-3272, 3294, 3464
- 문제: run_line_of_sight samples the ray with `elev, ok = provider.sample(...)` and drops any failure with a bare `continue`: `if not ok: continue` (3175-3176) and `if math.isnan(elev_value): continue` (3185-3186). QgsRasterDataProvider::sample returns ok=False both for a declared-NoData cell and for a point outside the raster, so the dropped samples are exactly the NoData ones. Nothing counts them, nothing warns. The two numbers that anchor the whole computation are then read off the *surviving* list: `obs_elev = profile_data[0]['elevation'] + obs_height` and `tgt_elev = profile_data[-1]['elevation'] + tgt_height` (3202-3203). When the head (or tail) of the ray is NoData, profile_data[0] is no longer the observer — it is the first valid cell further along — so the sight line is anchored at the w …
- 실패 시나리오: Observer clicked on a reservoir surface that the NGII DEM masks as NoData (or on a cell just outside a DEM clipped to the site polygon), target on a hilltop 1200 m away; the first ~200 m of the ray is NoData. The 40 leading samples are dropped. obs_elev becomes the elevation 200 m along (say 42 m) + 1.7 m = 43.7 m instead of the true 121 m + 1.7 m. Almost every subsequent terrain sample now sits above that sight line, so is_visible_overall goes False: the 가시선_Targets layer publishes the target as "안보임" and the 가시선_Observers layer as "보이는 대상 없음", for a target that is in plain view from the real …
- 제안 수정: Treat a failed sample as a failure, not as absence. Sample the observer and target cells explicitly first and abort with a message when either is NoData ("관측점/대상점이 NoData 영역에 있습니다"), matching what cost_surface_dialog.py:1073-1078 already does for its start/end points. Count the dropped intermediate samples, refuse (or clearly flag) when the ray crosses NoData rather than reporting a verdict over ground that was never tested, add a "샘플 실패" status category the way spatial_network's edge layer does …

#### [major] ND-03 — Terrain-profile statistics mix a NoData-truncated sample list with the full drawn length: 평균경사 uses the wrong denominator, the CSV's total_distance_m contradicts its own sample table, and unsampled segments are exported as perfectly flat

- 위치: `tools/terrain_profile_dialog.py` :2036-2041, 2679, 2727, 2747, 2827, 2854, 2912-2947
- 문제: Samples are appended only when `ok_val` is true, i.e. finite and not close to sourceNoDataValue (2015-2041), and each carries `dist = fraction * total_distance_m` — an absolute distance measured from the drawn start. So when the head of the line falls on NoData, profile_data starts at dists[0] > 0, and when the tail does, it ends short of the drawn length. Every consumer then mixes the two frames. update_stats: `total_d = float(dists[-1])` (2727) is printed as "총 거리", and `mean_abs_slope = ((ascent + descent) / total_d * 100.0)` (2747) divides a rise-and-fall accumulated only over [dists[0], dists[-1]] by a run measured from 0. export_csv repeats it: `total_d = float(dists[-1])` (2827) is written as the summary row `total_distance_m` (2854) while the per-sample table below it starts at dis …
- 실패 시나리오: A 1000 m profile drawn across a valley on a DEM clipped to the catchment, so the first 300 m of the line lies on the NoData collar. 500 samples, 구간 통계 on at 200 m. The message bar first says "시작점에서 끝점까지 1000.0m, 500개 샘플 추출 중", then "350개 유효 샘플 추출 완료". The stats label reads "총 거리: 1000.0m … 평균경사(|%|): 8.4%" — ascent+descent cover only 700 m of run but are divided by 1000, so the true figure over measured ground is 12.0% and the tool under-reports the mean slope by 30%. The exported CSV's summary block says total_distance_m = 1000.0 while its Distance(m) column starts at 300.0, and its segment t …
- 제안 수정: Carry the sampled span explicitly: use `span = dists[-1] - dists[0]` as the denominator for mean_abs_slope and as the exported run, and report both the drawn length and the sampled span rather than one number labelled "총 거리". Anchor the segment table at dists[0] (or emit the row with empty/NULL slope and ascent fields, plus a sample-count column, when no sample pair overlaps a segment) so an unmeasured segment cannot read as flat. Write valid_samples (not num_samples) into the `samples` field of …

#### [minor] ND-04 — LCP profile and milestone sampling bridge NoData gaps as one long straight edge, so the 직선 distance and time quoted against the least-cost path are wrong

- 위치: `tools/cost_surface_dialog.py` :3436-3446, 3484-3485 (same pattern at 3259-3266)
- 문제: sample_profile walks the densified coordinates at step_m ≈ one pixel and skips any point whose elevation is unavailable: `z = _bilinear_elevation(dem, nodata_mask, inv_win_gt, ...)` then `if z is None: continue` (3436-3438). x_prev/y_prev/z_prev are not reset on the skip, so the next valid point is joined to the last valid point before the gap: `horiz = math.hypot(float(x) - float(x_prev), ...)`, `dz = float(z) - float(z_prev)`, and `cum_time_s += _edge_cost(model_key, horiz, dz, model_params, cost_mode="time_s")` (3439-3444). One call to _edge_cost therefore prices the whole gap at the single average gradient dz/horiz, which for a symmetric obstacle is ~0 and yields flat-ground speed. _bilinear_elevation returns None both for a NoData neighbourhood (3007-3013) and for a point outside the …
- 실패 시나리오: Two sites on opposite banks of a river, DEM with the channel masked as NoData over a 150 m width (standard for NGII water bodies). Model Tobler, pixel 5 m, LCP computed, then 프로파일 opened. The ~30 densified points over the channel return None; sample_profile joins the bank-top before the channel to the bank-top after it as a single 155 m edge whose dz is the ~0 m net difference between the two bank tops, so Tobler prices 155 m at full flat-ground speed (~1.9 min) instead of the real descent-and-climb of 20 m each way (~3.3 min). The dialog header reads "LCP 2.40km / 31.2분 | 직선 1.90km / 21.4분" — …
- 제안 수정: Reset x_prev/y_prev/z_prev to None when a sample is skipped so the gap starts a new run rather than being bridged, accumulate the skipped length separately, and either (a) refuse to report a 직선/LCP comparison when a gap exists, or (b) show the gap length in the header and in the milestone labels ("직선 1.90km / 21.4분 (DEM 결측 155m 제외)"). Apply the same change in _create_lcp_milestones_layer so a milestone's cumulative time never includes a bridged gap silently.

#### [minor] ND-05 — When the circular-mask clip produces no file the single-point viewshed silently falls back to the unmasked raster, turning the out-of-range collar from NoData into a real "보이지 않음" over the whole DEM

- 위치: `tools/viewshed_dialog.py` :2015-2016 (with 2002-2013, 4274, 1830)
- 문제: The single-point path clips the raw gdal:viewshed output to the max-distance circle with gdal:cliprasterbymasklayer, NODATA -9999, DATA_TYPE 6 (2002-2013) — the step the comment at 2002-2003 says is what makes the outside "absolutely transparent". If that call returns without writing a file the code does `if not os.path.exists(final_output): shutil.copy(raw_output, final_output)` (2015-2016) and carries on with no message, no log line and no metadata flag. QGIS's GDAL provider does not raise on a gdalwarp that fails at runtime (an invalid cutline, a degenerate buffer ring, a CRS mismatch on the memory mask layer) — it returns normally with no OUTPUT — so this branch is reachable. The copied raster is the raw byte viewshed over the whole DEM: gdal_viewshed writes its out-of-range value (0) …
- 실패 시나리오: Single-point viewshed, max_dist 5000 m, on a DEM whose CRS the memory mask layer cannot be cut against (the buffer ring degenerates, gdalwarp exits with "Cutline polygon is invalid" and writes nothing). The user sees a pink "보이지 않음" field covering the entire DEM instead of a 5 km disc — a claim that every cell out to the DEM edge was analysed and found not visible. With AOI 통계 ticked, _compute_aoi_visibility_stats_layer's tot_px (1830) now counts the AOI's whole footprint rather than only its intersection with the 5 km disc, so vis …
- 제안 수정: Make the fallback loud and honest: when final_output is missing after the clip, either abort with a message ("원형 마스크 적용에 실패했습니다") or, if the unmasked raster is still worth showing, name the layer accordingly, set a params flag such as {"circular_mask_applied": False} in set_archtoolkit_layer_metadata, and skip the AOI statistic (or state in its message that the radius mask was not applied). ### sweep-claims


#### [blocker] CLAIM-01 — "누적값을 '개수(1~N)'로 표시" is labelled and tooltipped as a legend option, but unchecked it silently replaces the count raster with a binary union above 20 observer points

- 위치: `tools/viewshed_dialog.py` :4105
- 문제: tools/viewshed_dialog_base.ui:471-477 gives chkCountOnly the text "누적값을 '개수(1~N)'로 표시" and the tooltip "체크 시 범례가 V(1,2,3...) 조합 대신 '보이지 않음 / 1개 누적 / 2개 누적 / … / N개 누적' 형태로 표시됩니다" — i.e. it presents itself as a display/legend choice, and it ships unchecked (<bool>false</bool> at ui:479, re-forced False at viewshed_dialog.py:714). radioMultiPoint's tooltip (ui:80) promises the mode "여러 관측점의 가시권을 합산하여 '얼마나 많은 지점에서 보이는지' 계산합니다". In fact that checkbox is the only thing that decides whether the raster holds observer COUNTS or a binary 0/255 union whenever there are more than 20 observer points or the mode is line-viewshed: with it unchecked, is_union_mode becomes True and every visible cell is written as 255, destroying the count information entirely. The resulting layer is still named 가시권_누적_{N …
- 실패 시나리오: 45 hilltop fort sites loaded as the observer point layer, 다중 관측점 mode, 최대거리 3000 m, all other settings default (chkCountOnly unchecked, chkWeightedCumulative unchecked). len(points)=45 > 20 → is_union_mode=True. Every visible cell in the output is 255; no cell records how many of the 45 forts see it. The layer added to the project is named "가시권_누적_45개점" and the user, who read the checkbox tooltip and concluded it only restyles the legend, puts that raster in the report as a cumulative-visibility map. Ticking the box produces a genuinely different raster (values 1..45). Same run with 20 observe …

#### [major] CLAIM-03 — The 경사도 단계(°) setting changes neither the number of zones nor the number of labels its tooltip promises to reduce — dissolve always merges on the 1° value

- 위치: `tools/slope_aspect_drafting_dialog.py` :553

Neither half is true. native:dissolve is keyed on slope_deg (the value rounded to a whole degree), never on slope_class, so the polygon count is fixed at whatever the 1° merge yields regardless of the setting — the code's own comment and the output layer name "경사도_구역(1°)" say so. And the label placed on each polygon is f"{slope_deg}°", a single integer degree, with displayAll=True and allowOverlap=True, so the label count equals the polygon count and is likewise independent of the setting. slope_class_step affects only the categorised fill colours and the legend entry text. A user who raises 경사도 단계 sp …
- 실패 시나리오: 500 m × 500 m AOI on a 5 m DEM, 표시 간격 5 셀, slopes ranging 0–40°. At 경사도 단계 = 1° the dissolve produces N zone polygons carrying N labels ("0°", "1°", … "40°"). The user sets 경사도 단계 = 10° expecting ~4 zones and ~4 labels. The run produces exactly the same N polygons and the same N labels reading "0°", "1°" … "40°"; only the legend collapses from ~41 colour entries to 5 ("0~10°", "10~20°", …). Nothing on the printed sheet is cleaner.
- 제안 수정: Dissolve on slope_class instead of slope_deg (FIELD: ["slope_class"]), compute slope_class before the dissolve rather than in the styling pass, set the label field to the class range (f"{v0}~{v1}°") rather than the per-cell degree, and rename the output layer to carry the actual step (경사도_구역({step}°)). If the 1° merge is intentional, rewrite the tooltip to say the setting controls colour classes and the legend only, and drop the "구역 수/라벨이 줄어들어" claim.

#### [major] CLAIM-06 — cost-network's closeness tooltip and help state a monotonicity the implementation does not have, and credit Freeman (1979) for a Wasserman–Faust statistic — the twin spatial-network tool discloses both correctly

- 위치: `tools/cost_network_dialog.py` :1442
- 문제: closeness_centrality_weighted applies the Wasserman–Faust component-size correction, multiplying by reachable/(n−1). The score therefore depends on how much of the network a node can reach, not only on its cost sum, and the stated ordering can invert. Both user-facing surfaces in this tool assert the uncorrected Freeman ordering, and the field alias set at line 2551 is a bare "근접 중심성(closeness)" with nothing to distinguish it. The twin tool gets this right — tools/spatial_network_dialog.py:742-744 says "Wasserman–Faust 보정 적용: 도달 가능한 노드 비율(r/(n−1))을 곱해, 고립된 소규모 컴포넌트가 만점을 받는 왜곡을 제거했습니다" — so this is an unclosed twin-tool gap, and the cited reference is wrong for the formula implemented (network_metrics.py's own module docstring names Wasserman–Faust).
- 실패 시나리오: 10 sites, k-NN cost network, closeness enabled. Site A sits in a 2-site isolated component joined by a single 1-minute edge: reachable=1, sum=1, closeness = (1/1)·(1/9) = 0.111. Site B sits inside the main 8-site cluster with shortest-cost distances to its 7 reachable peers summing to 20 minutes: closeness = (7/20)·(7/9) = 0.272. B's "최단 비용 합" is 20× A's, yet B scores 2.4× higher. A user ranking sites by the tooltip's rule concludes A is the most accessible site in the study area and writes that into the report; the attribute table says the opposite. The same user cites Freeman (1979) for the …
- 제안 수정: Replace the tooltip and the help bullet with the spatial-network wording: state the Wasserman–Faust correction, that the score scales with the fraction of the network reached, and that values from graphs with different component structures are not directly comparable. Change the reference to Wasserman & Faust (1994) (keeping Freeman 1979 as the origin of the uncorrected measure if desired), and extend the field alias to "근접 중심성(closeness, W–F 보정)" so an exported attribute table carries the quali …

#### [minor] CLAIM-07 — Aspect layer name and tooltip claim an 8-compass-direction classification, but the classes are the eight bins bounded by those directions — no class is N, NE, E, SE, S, SW, W or NW

- 위치: `tools/terrain_analysis_dialog.py` :113
- 문제: tools/terrain_analysis_dialog_base.ui:51 (chkAspect) states "사면이 향하는 방향을 8방위(N, NE, E, SE, S, SW, W, NW)로 분류합니다" and the layer name asserts 8방위. A standard 8-direction aspect classification centres each bin on its azimuth (N = 337.5–22.5°, NE = 22.5–67.5°, …). These bins are instead bounded at 0/45/90/…, so each class straddles two compass directions and no class corresponds to any single one. The legend labels themselves are honest about the ranges — that is the mitigation — but the tooltip and the layer name are not, and it is the layer name that ends up in a figure caption. Secondary defect in the same table: ZERO_FLAT=True (line 431) writes flat cells as 0, and gdaldem's azimuth aspect also returns exactly 0 for a due-north-facing cell (it maps 360.0 to 0.0), so on an integer-valued DE …
- 실패 시나리오: Excavation report wants the standard "남향(S) 사면 비율" table for a hillslope site. A due-south slope at azimuth 180.0° falls exactly on the boundary between 'SE-S | 135~180°' and 'S-SW | 180~225°'. Neither class is S: taking 'S-SW' reports a band centred on SSW (202.5°), 22.5° off south and including due-west-of-south ground; taking both gives a 90° band from SE to SW. A reviewer comparing the figure against any conventional 8-direction aspect table (S = 157.5–202.5°) gets a different number from the same DEM, and the layer caption "사면방향_8방위" gives no warning that the binning differs.
- 제안 수정: Either re-bin to the conventional scheme (shift the breaks to 22.5/67.5/112.5/… with the N class wrapping 337.5–360 plus 0–22.5) and keep the 8방위 wording, or keep the current bins and fix the wording: rename the layer to 사면방향_45°구간 (평탄=0) and change the chkAspect tooltip to enumerate the actual classes (N-NE, NE-E, …) instead of the eight compass names. Separately, give flat cells their own sentinel instead of sharing 0 with due north (e.g. keep gdaldem's -9999 for flats and style NoData), or re …

#### [minor] CLAIM-08 — Slope-drafting legend breaks are half a degree off their printed labels because the slope is rounded to whole degrees before it is binned

- 위치: `tools/slope_aspect_drafting_dialog.py` :507
- 문제: The gdal:slope value is rounded to the nearest integer degree first, and the class is then derived from that integer. A class labelled "{v0}~{v1}°" therefore contains slopes in [v0−0.5, v1−0.5), not [v0, v1). Every break in the legend of the printed drawing — and every value in the slope_class attribute — is displaced by half a degree. The same rounding is repeated independently in _apply_slope_grid_style (line 672-678), so the attribute and the legend agree with each other and disagree with the DEM.
- 실패 시나리오: 경사도 단계 = 5° (the shipped default), so the legend prints classes "0~5°", "5~10°", "10~15°", "15~20°". A grid block whose sampled gdal:slope value is 14.7° is rounded to 15, binned to class 15, and rendered and labelled under "15~20°". A block at 19.6° is rounded to 20 and moves out of "15~20°" into "20~25°". On a drawing used to argue that a terrace falls below the 15° threshold, ground measured at 14.7° is presented as being in the 15–20° class.
- 제안 수정: Bin the unrounded value: slope_class = int(math.floor(slope / cls_step) * cls_step), clamped to [0, 90], and keep slope_deg (the rounded value) only for the per-polygon text label. Apply the same change in _apply_slope_grid_style so the recomputed class matches, or better, stop recomputing it there and read the value written at build time.

#### [major] SILENT-04 — Viewshed AOI statistics compute vis_pct and tot_m2 over only the part of the AOI inside the analysis circle, with no note on the layer, the label or the message

The AOI statistics then clamp their read window to the raster and mask off NoData (`valid = np.isfinite(arr)`, `valid &= (arr != nodata)`), so tot_px counts only AOI cells that lie inside the analysis circle. tot_m2 is therefore not the AOI's area and vis_pct is not the visible fraction of the AOI - both are relative to AOI-intersect-circle. Nothing says so: the fields carry no aliases, the polygon label is `round("vis_pct", 1) || '%'` (line 1860), the message bar reads "가시비율 62.3% | 가시면적 X m² / Y m²" (line 1942), the .ui hint says only "※ 결과는 'AOI_가시통계' 레이어로 추가됩니다" (tools/viewshed_dialog_base.ui:603), and the help …
- 실패 시나리오: Observer on a hilltop at the south-west corner of a 2 km x 2 km survey AOI, max_dist = 1000 m. The circle covers a quarter-disc of the AOI: pi*1000^2/4 = 785,398 m^2 of the AOI's 4,000,000 m^2. Say 60% of that quarter-disc is visible = 471,239 m^2. The AOI_가시통계 layer records tot_m2 = 785,398 (for a polygon whose real area is 4,000,000), vis_m2 = 471,239, vis_pct = 60.0, the map label reads "60%", and the message bar reads "가시비율 60.0% | 가시면적 471239 m² / 785398 m²". The true visible fraction of the survey area is 471,239 / 4,000,000 = 11.8%. The report sentence "60% of the survey area is intervi …
- 제안 수정: Measure the AOI polygon's full area independently (QgsDistanceArea on geom_dem) and write it as a separate field, e.g. aoi_m2, next to tot_m2; rename tot_m2/vis_pct to something that says what the denominator is (analysed_m2, vis_pct_of_analysed) or add field aliases and a second percentage against aoi_m2. Set an out-of-range indicator when tot_m2 < aoi_m2 and say so in the message bar, and expose visible_threshold (or record it in the layer metadata params alongside raster_name/aoi_layer at lin …

#### [major] SILENT-05 — Terrain profile drops NoData samples and then draws, smooths, and computes statistics straight across the gap with no break or count in the result

- 위치: `tools/terrain_profile_dialog.py` :2029-2041 (samples dropped), 515 (line drawn across the gap), 160-168 (smoothing spans the gap), 2734-2747 (ascent/descent/max slope), 2805-2860 (CSV)
- 문제: Samples whose DEM value is NoData or non-finite are skipped and never recorded anywhere except the count in a transient message bar ("{valid_samples}개 유효 샘플 추출 완료!", line 2072). The chart's QPainterPath joins the last sample before the gap to the first after it with a straight segment; the +-3 sample moving average is index-based so it also blends elevations from opposite sides of the gap; update_stats' ascent/descent/max-slope loop (line 2734) walks the same consecutive-kept-sample list; and export_csv simply omits the rows, so nothing downstream can tell a gap from flat ground. The disclosure the tool does make - "차트 곡선: ±3점 이동평균(통계/CSV는 원시값)" (line 2799) - covers the smoothing, not the interpolation across missing data.
- 실패 시나리오: A 2 km profile is drawn across a reservoir where the DEM has a void; 60 of 201 requested samples fall in the void. The chart draws a clean straight segment ~600 m long across the void, the exported PNG that goes into the excavation report reads as a flat terrace, and the stats line reports 총 거리 2000.0 m with 누적상승/누적하강 that omit all relief under the void. The CSV has 141 rows with a 600 m jump between two consecutive `distance` values and no flag. The only hint is that a message bar which disappears after a few seconds said 141 while the spinbox said 200.
- 제안 수정: Record a `nodata` or `gap` marker in profile_data (or keep the sample with elevation=None), start a new QPainterPath subpath at each gap so the curve visibly breaks, reset the smoothing window at gap boundaries, exclude gap-spanning segments from ascent/descent/max-slope, emit the skipped rows in the CSV with an empty elevation, and append the dropped count to the persistent stats line (e.g. "| NoData 60/201 구간 제외") rather than only to the message bar.

#### [minor] SILENT-06 — When the circular clip of a viewshed fails, the uncropped full-DEM raster is substituted silently, changing what every downstream AOI percentage means

- 위치: `tools/viewshed_dialog.py` :2016 (run_single_viewshed) and 2174 (_compute_viewshed_raster_file)
- 문제: Both clip sites fall back to the raw gdal:viewshed output when the masked file is missing. The raw output spans the whole DEM and marks cells beyond MAX_DISTANCE as not-visible (value 0) rather than NoData, so the substituted raster has a different extent and a different NoData convention from the one every downstream consumer assumes. Nothing records the substitution - not a log_message, not the layer name (which still reads "가시권_단일점_{max_dist}m"), not the layer metadata params (max_dist_m is stamped regardless).
- 실패 시나리오: gdal:cliprasterbymasklayer fails on a machine whose GDAL rejects the in-memory mask layer. run_single_viewshed copies the raw raster through. _add_aoi_stats_layer_for_raster (line 2078) then reads it: because the out-of-range cells are 0 rather than -9999, `valid` is True for them, so tot_px now counts the WHOLE AOI instead of the AOI-intersect-circle. The same site, same AOI, same max_dist reported 60.0% on a machine where the clip worked and 11.8% on this one, and the two runs are indistinguishable from the layer, its name, its metadata and the message bar.
- 제안 수정: Treat a missing final_output as an error, or at minimum log a Warning, push a message bar, and stamp a flag (e.g. params["circular_clip"] = False) so the AOI-statistics path and any reader can tell which convention the raster follows.

#### [minor] SILENT-07 — The AHP AOI option uses the bounding RECTANGLE of the AOI polygons while the guide states statistics are computed only inside the AOI

- 위치: `tools/aoi_extent.py` :180 (rect = geom.boundingBox()); consumed at tools/ahp_suitability_dialog.py:1645-1662 and 2172-2181; claimed at docs/AHP_GUIDE.md:263
- 문제: resolve_aoi_extent unions the AOI polygons and returns their bounding box, and _compute_minmax_for_layer passes that rectangle to bandStatistics. The polygon itself never masks anything - the dialog's own _processing_clip_raster_by_mask helper (line 1747) has no call site. The guide's sentence promises polygon-level containment, and it is the sentence the guide uses to persuade the user that this option protects them from the stretched-ramp failure it describes two lines earlier.
- 실패 시나리오: A survey AOI that is a 20 km NE-SW road corridor 100 m wide (~2 km^2). Its bounding box is roughly 14 km x 14 km = 196 km^2, i.e. ~100x the corridor. With 'AOI 범위로 자르기' checked, the elevation criterion's min/max are taken over that 196 km^2 box, which includes ridge tops several kilometres from the corridor. Every scoring ramp is stretched to a range the corridor never reaches, the whole corridor scores near the bottom of the ramp, and the resulting suitability map is exactly the failure the guide's own warning box describes - while the checkbox the user ticked to prevent it is reported in par …
- 제안 수정: Either call the existing _processing_clip_raster_by_mask on each aligned criterion (and compute statistics with QgsZonalStatistics or a masked read) so the polygon really bounds the numbers, or change the guide sentence and the checkbox label to say 외접 사각형/bounding box, and add a hint when the union's area is much smaller than its bounding box (e.g. ratio < 0.5) telling the user the statistics span far more ground than their AOI.

#### [minor] SILENT-08 — A failed non-align AOI clip leaves the AHP output unclipped while the layer metadata still records clip_to_aoi_extent: true

- 위치: `tools/ahp_suitability_dialog.py` :2344-2362 (silent fallback) and 2050 (the metadata claim)
- 문제: The non-align clip is best-effort: an exception, or a processing run that produces no file, leaves acc_path pointing at the full-extent accumulator and the run continues. log_exception (tools/utils.py:310-322) writes to the QGIS log panel only - no message bar - while params_json unconditionally records clip_to_aoi_extent as the checkbox state rather than what actually happened. The statistics used to build the ramps were still AOI-only, so the pixels outside the AOI are scored against an AOI range and saturate at exactly 0.0 or 1.0 through ahp_core.clamp01.
- 실패 시나리오: Four pre-aligned covariate rasters covering a province; user unticks '첫 번째 기준 레이어에 정렬' because the grids already match, leaves 'AOI 범위로 자르기' ticked, and the gdal:cliprasterbyextent step fails (locked temp file, PROJWIN CRS token rejected). The saved GeoTIFF covers the whole province. Outside the AOI, every criterion pixel beyond the AOI's min/max is clamped, so large regions come out as flat 1.0 - reading as perfect suitability - and the layer's params_json asserts clip_to_aoi_extent: true. A reviewer checking provenance is told the map was clipped to the survey area when it was not.
- 제안 수정: On the exception or missing-file branch, push a level-1 message bar naming the failure and set params["clip_to_aoi_extent"] from the outcome (a local flag) rather than from chkClipToAoiExtent.isChecked(); or abort the run the way the AOI-statistics failure path at line 2145-2150 already does.

#### [minor] SILENT-09 — Isochrone and iso-energy contour sets are capped at 60 / 80 levels, so a high-cost surface gets contours over only part of its range

- 위치: `tools/cost_models.py` :231-235 (isochrone_levels_minutes) and 273-277 (isoenergy_levels_kcal); consumed at tools/cost_surface_dialog.py:1361 and 1406
- 문제: The cap is enforced silently inside the level generator. The layer that results is named "등시간선 (Isochrones)" / "등에너지선 (Iso-energy)" (tools/cost_surface_dialog.py:2624, 2639) with no note of the range it actually covers, and _tag_cost_surface_layer stamps only model / cost_mode / connectivity. A reader of the map cannot tell a genuinely unreachable area from one whose contours were simply not emitted, because both look the same: no contour line.
- 실패 시나리오: A rugged 5 m DEM where the accumulated-cost maximum is 6000 minutes (reachable once the SILENT-01 floor charges 20 s per metre over a few thousand steep cells, or with a friction multiplier). isochrone_levels_minutes emits 15/30/45/60, 90/120/150/180, then 240 up to 3300 - 60 levels - and stops. The isochrone layer covers 0-3300 min; everything from 3300 to 6000 min carries no contour at all. A reader interprets the blank outer band as beyond the analysis rather than as the most expensive half of the surface, and no layer name, legend entry or metadata field corrects them.
- 제안 수정: Return the fact that the cap bound (e.g. a second return value or a sentinel level at max) and have cost_surface_dialog append it to the layer name and to approx_params, e.g. "등시간선 (0–3300분, 상한 도달)"; or widen the spacing once the cap is approached so the full range is always covered within 60 levels.

#### [minor] SILENT-10 — Slope-grid drafting polygons are labelled with a single centre-cell slope presented as the block's slope

- 위치: `tools/slope_aspect_drafting_dialog.py` :493-496 (sampling) and 534-537 (attributes written)
- 문제: Each output polygon spans step_cells x step_cells DEM cells but carries the slope of one cell at its centre, written to fields named slope / slope_deg / slope_class and drawn as a label "{slope_deg}°" over the whole square (tools/slope_aspect_drafting_dialog.py:648-678). No mean, maximum or range is computed or stored. The .ui text discloses density only - the top help says slope is "단계(예: 0~5°, 5~10°…)로 구간화" (tools/slope_aspect_drafting_dialog_base.ui:26) and the 표시 간격 tooltip says "5 = 5셀마다 1개" (line 144) - neither states that the printed figure is one cell's value rather than the block's.
- 실패 시나리오: 5 m DEM, 표시 간격 = 10, so each polygon covers a 50 m x 50 m block. In dissected terrain the centre cell of one block sits on a small bench at 3 degrees while the rest of the block runs 25-40 degrees. The drafted slope map prints "3°" across the whole 50 m square and colours it at the bottom of the Reds ramp. That figure goes into an excavation report as the slope of that part of the site, and nothing in the layer, the attribute table or the tooltip warns that it is a point sample.
- 제안 수정: Compute the block's mean (and optionally min/max) from arr[row:row2, col:col2] instead of one cell, or keep the point sample but rename the fields (slope_sample_deg) and say so in the 표시 간격 tooltip and the layer name, e.g. "경사도_격자 (중심셀 표본)". Storing slope_min/slope_max alongside would let a reader see when a block is not homogeneous.

#### [major] M3 — AHP suitability params_json records the criterion's scoring MODE but not the values that define the score curve (target_v / prefer_min / prefer_max / score_ranges)

- 위치: `/home/user/archtoolkit/tools/ahp_suitability_dialog.py` :2037-2048 (compare 1912-1921 and tools/ahp_core.py:335-400)
- 문제: _Criterion carries five scoring inputs (direction, target_v, prefer_min, prefer_max, score_ranges) and ahp_core.score_formula uses all of them to build the gdal_calc expression. The metadata stores only direction, min, max and weight. For direction="target" the position of the optimum is lost; for "range" the preferred interval is lost; for "reclass" the entire interval->score table - the whole definition of that criterion - is lost. Recording `"direction": "target"` with no target is worse than recording nothing, because the per-criterion block looks complete. docs/AHP_GUIDE.md:268-270 warns that changing min/max changes the map and tells the user to pin them; target_v/prefer_* have exactly the same effect on every pixel and are not pinned anywhere, while the module's own docstring at ahp …
- 실패 시나리오: Two AHP runs over the same DEM criterion (min=0, max=800, weight=0.5) with direction="target": run A target_v=120, run B target_v=400. A 120 m cell scores 1.00 in A and (800-120)/(800-400)=clamped 1.0... concretely a 200 m cell scores (800-200)/(800-120)=0.88 in A and (200-0)/(400-0)=0.50 in B - different suitability maps. Both output layers carry the identical params_json `{"criteria":[{"layer_id":"dem_...","direction":"target","min":0.0,"max":800.0,"weight":0.5,...}],"consistency_ratio":0.03,...}`. Six months later, with both rasters in one project, neither the layer, nor the AI report that …
- 제안 수정: Include the mode's own values in each criterion block: target_v for "target", prefer_min/prefer_max for "range", the full score_ranges list for "reclass" (all are plain floats/dicts, so JSON-safe) - ideally by storing the resolved values actually used, since _ensure_criterion_preference_defaults (line 1215-1260) may substitute defaults the user never typed.
